### Lying down

On a lounger or a water bed you lie back instead of sitting: the seat is an ordinary seat (E, W A S D to get up, `sit` on the wire as before), and `src/shared/lounging.ts` (`lieOn(seatId)`: the roof's loungers and the gym basement's quiet room, salt grotto and poolside loungers recline, its water beds are flat) says which. `src/client/features/lounging/` lays the body over it each frame (`liePose` through `Person.setWorkout`), yours and everyone else's from their `seat`; the seat's hint says "Lie down" / "lying back" (`features/seating/index.ts`, one fork line each). Installed in `main.ts` (`installLounging`). Gym seats carry an invisible pick box (`seatable` in `world/gym/kit.ts`), since their pieces are merged and lose their `userData.interact`.

### Swimming

One controller for every pool (`client/swim/`, `Swimmer`), the pools as data (`src/shared/swim.ts`, `PoolDef`), so a new basin (the thermal baths' are next) is a `PoolDef` and nothing more. A pool is the water over one or more rectangles (a freeform basin is several; their seams are no walls), its surface and floor, how deep a swimmer's feet hang (`sink`), the deck you climb out onto, how near a wall you may climb out (`edge`), how it swims (`speed`/`fast`, `jumpScale`, `dunk`, `strokeEvery`), where its walls are tried (`wallsAt`: your own height, or just over the roof's floor for a raised pool whose deck is a slab you're down in), its own way out if it has one (`climbOut`: the lap pool never onto a starting block, the roof's not under its slide), timed `lengths` along an axis, and for later `swell` (waves lift the surface, on the office's clock) and `flow` (a current carries you; carried off its water you're back on your feet). The pure maths is beside it: `overPool` (in from the union's edge), `inPoolAt` (who's posed swimming), `atEdge`, `climbOutAt` (straight out over the nearest wall with dry deck behind it), `poolAt`, `surfaceAt`, `wallTouched`. The controller: `tick` (in once you're over the water and under its surface, harder the higher you came from), `jumpIn` (a header toward the nearest stretch), `into`, `climbOut`, `leave`, the lengths with your best in `localStorage`, `pose` (everyone in any of its pools swimming: `seaPose`), `release`. **No new message types**: every page works out who's swimming from where they are.

- The lap pool (`LAP_SWIM` in `shared/gym-basement.ts`) and the roof's pool (`ROOF_SWIM` in `shared/roofpool.ts`) are on it, as they were: `GymPool` and `PoolSwim` (`features/roofpool/swim.ts`) are thin subclasses keeping their old faces (`swimming`/`active`, `enter`, `atEdge`, `climbOut`, ...); the sea (`features/beach/swim.ts`) has its own, an open sea being no pool.
- `tests/pool-swim.test.ts`: both pools as data agree with the old functions point for point (over, in, at the edge, out), a freeform L (its seam, its corners, out always onto dry deck), lengths, a timed length in the lap pool and climbing out, a header in, the roof's walls, waves and a current.

### The padel hall

Across the street to the east (x 22..54, z 36..72, where the neighbour at x 30 stood), the casino's counterpart: a padel hall with a gallery and a café. A pale grey sports hall under a curved roof, a big glass front with the warm-lit courts showing through, PADEL in lime LED letters with a racket and a ball, a canopy over glass doors that slide apart, bike racks, benches and a flag. **E** at the doors goes in; **E** at the doors inside comes back out onto the street of the floor you came from. Inside is a place of its own like the casino (`HALL = '@hall'` is your `floor` while you're in there), so people from every floor meet, see and hear each other there (guests and party guests too: going in and out is `floor.go`, sitting `sit`, a café item `act`, so there's nothing new to sort in `guests.ts` or `party.ts`). The building's half is below; the padel game's (courts, glass, fence, net, lines, court lights, the game) is its own section. It adds itself to the room with `hall.add({ build })` (`client/hall.ts`, called from `features/places`), nothing of the building stands on a court or within `COURT_MARGIN` of one.

- The shared plan: `src/shared/hall.ts` (`HALL`, the room, the courts, the entry and the inside door, the gallery, `COURT_MARGIN`; plus the building on the street: `HALL_BOX`, `HALL_HEIGHT`, `HALL_ROOF_RISE`, `HALL_DOOR`, `HALL_STREET_SPOT`).
- Own files (the building and the café):
  - `src/shared/hall-building.ts`: where the furniture stands inside (the stairs a step at a time, the gallery's pillars, the stand's rows and risers, reception, lockers, the door bench, the café's counter and tables), `HALL_SEATING` (the stand's benches, the door bench, the café chairs, all `hall: true`), `COURT_KEEP_OUT` / `onCourt`.
  - `src/shared/cafe.ts`: the café's menu (`CAFE_ITEMS`: coffees with the kitchen machine's buzz, cold drinks, cakes and bakes), `isCafeItem`.
  - `src/server/hall.ts`: `hallView`, `HALL_ARRIVAL`, `backInHall`.
  - `src/client/hall.ts` (`HallPlace`: in and out, the room, the café's counter, hints, the light inside, and `add(part)` for the padel game), `src/client/ui/cafe.ts` (the menu), `src/client/world/hall/` (`exterior.ts`, also the block the roof's city shows; `interior.ts`; `cafeitems.ts`, the cups, glasses and plates in your hand).
  - `tests/hall-building.test.ts` (the lot, the doors, the courts kept clear, the stairs, the café, the seats only in the hall), `tests/cafe.test.ts` (the menu, what the server lets you hold where).

  - `src/shared/layout.ts`: `SeatDef.hall`, `...HALL_SEATING` in `SEATING`, `seatHere(key, onRoof, inHall)`. `src/shared/maps/index.ts`: `seatHereOn(..., inHall)`.

### Speakers all over the office

Small speakers hang from the ceiling round the office and play whatever the jukebox plays, so it's heard all over the floor, not only in the lounge: over both desk clusters, by the boards, between the whiteboard and the elevator, in the lounge, the kitchen, by the balcony doors, in the meeting room and the loft (smaller, under their low ceilings), and one per row of the back office once it's built out. Their LED glows green and the woofer pumps with the beat while music plays; red when the floor has them off. You hear them inside the office on your floor, a little on the balcony and fire escape, and not in the garage, the street or on the roof.

They're one PA, not a source per speaker (no piling up, no phasing): the tune goes into the jukebox's panner as before and into a speaker bus (a bit thinner, like small boxes), whose level is the nearest speaker's by distance, lightly panned towards the nearest two. A radio stream stays one audio element, at the louder of the jukebox where you stand and the speakers. **⚙️ → Sound & voice → Speakers** is your own volume (default 40 %, saved with the other settings); muting the jukebox mutes them too. The jukebox's window has quick steps (✕ ▁ ▁▃ ▁▃▅), and for the team a switch that turns them off on the floor for everyone (saved in jukebox.json).

- Own files: `src/client/speakers.ts` (where they hang, the level math, the steps), `src/client/world/speakers.ts` (the boxes), `src/client/sound/speakers.ts` (`Speakers`: the speaker bus), `src/client/features/speakers/index.ts` (built into the office, `setSpeakerRoom(...)` and updated each frame), `src/client/ui/speakers.ts` and `src/client/ui/speakers.css` (the row in the jukebox's window); `tests/speakers.test.ts`.
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/client/features/jukebox/sound.ts`: the tune goes into `musicSrc` (which feeds the jukebox's panner and the speakers), a speakers section (`setSpeakerVolume`, `hearSpeakers`), `hearStream` uses `streamVolume`. `src/client/sound/index.ts`: `setSpeakerVolume`, `setSpeakerRoom`, `speakerLevel` in the fork's section.
  - `src/client/state/persist.ts`: `Settings.speakers`/`speakersMuted`, loaded and saved (`tests/client-store.test.ts` knows them). `src/client/ui/settings.ts`: the Speakers row.
  - `src/client/features/jukebox/ui.ts`: `openJukebox(..., speakerControl)` puts the speakers' row in; `src/client/features/jukebox/index.ts` passes the control.
  - `src/client/core/you.ts` (at start) and `src/client/features/hud/index.ts` (on a settings change): `setSpeakerVolume` next to `setMusicVolume`. `src/client/main.ts`: `installSpeakers(...)`.
  - `src/shared/jukebox.ts`: `JukeboxState.speakersOff`. `src/server/jukebox.ts`: `setSpeakers()`, kept through new tunes, saved and loaded.
  - `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts` (fork file): the `jukebox.speakers` message; its handler (toast to the floor) in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`. `src/server/guests.ts`: `jukebox.speakers` is `TEAM_ONLY`.
  - `docs/features.md`: the speakers line.

### Flogge's own car

Florian's own car stands in the garage: **Flogge's Bulli**, a split-window camper van (teal below, cream above with the V down its nose, round headlights, whitewalls, a surfboard on the roof rack, FLOGGE on the plates front and back), backed into the east corner of the back wall under a sign of its own, with lines round its spot. Only its keyholders take the wheel; everyone else hears "That's Flogge's Bulli — ask him for a ride" and may sit in the passenger seat. The server enforces it at `car.enter`, whatever a page sends. It starts slower than the supercars but pulls on to 200 km/h (steering calms down past 80 km/h so it stays drivable), rocks on its springs, and its horn is an old buzzy "möp möp". Its wing mirrors work: while you sit in it, each glass shows what's behind (`src/client/features/cars/mirrors.ts`, a small render target per mirror, one mirror drawn per frame).

Who holds the keys is set, not written in: `.agent-office/car-keys.json` lists account ids, set with `agent-office car keys <name>...` (`agent-office car` shows them, `agent-office car keys --admins` goes back to the default), picked up while the office runs. With nobody named, every admin (and the shared office password) holds them. Each page learns whether it may drive from `Me.bulli`.

Driving somewhere comes next: `src/shared/destinations.ts` is where named places to drive to (the supermarket, first) go, with the steps for adding one: its own lot in `PAVEMENT` (or off the scenic loop), listed there, drawn, and named in the drive hint.

- Own files: `src/shared/bulli.ts` (its driving, seats, heights, who may take which seat), `src/server/carkeys.ts` (the keys and the `agent-office car` command), `src/client/world/bulli.ts` (the van, its plates, its springs, its corner), `src/client/sound/bulli.ts` (`bulliHorn`), `src/client/features/cars/mirrors.ts` (the wing mirrors), `src/shared/destinations.ts`; `tests/bulli.test.ts`.
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/shared/garage.ts`: `CarKind` has `'bulli'`, `CarDef.owned`/`plate`, the Bulli in `CARS` (last, so no other car's index moves), `DriveTuning`, `drive(..., t)`, `steerLimit(..., t)` (and its extra taper past 22 m/s), `tuningOf`, `seatsOf`, `hipsOf`, `heightOf`.

  - `src/shared/protocol/accounts.ts`: `Me.bulli`.
  - `src/server/fork/office.ts`: `carKeys` on the `Fork` context; `src/server/fork/roles.ts`: the keys in `roleOf` (`meOf`, `src/server/office/people.ts`), `Client.bulli` passed on like `admin`/`guest` (`roleMoved`/`roleChanged`, in `office/people.ts` and `office/timers.ts`). `src/server/office/client.ts`: `Client.bulli`; `src/server/ws/connection.ts`: set at connecting. `src/server/ws/handlers/car.ts`: the refusal in `car.enter`.
  - `src/server/cli.ts`: the `car` command; `src/server/config.ts`: its line in the help.

### Racing rig

A racing rig in the lounge, next to the arcade cabinet: out between the lounge and the meeting room's glass (x 14.2–15.6, z 5.9–8.0), a bucket seat on an aluminium frame, a wheel, pedals and a TV on a stand, facing the glass so the lounge sees its screen. **E** there sits you down (hands on the wheel, for everyone to see), the camera glides up to the TV, and you race OFFICE GP: a pseudo-3D arcade racer, three laps against five CPU cars (CLAUDE, CODEX, GROK, GEMINI, OPENCODE) after a 3-2-1 countdown, lap timer, best lap, off-road slowdown, bumps, a boost meter. Arrows or WASD, Space boost, R restart, a gamepad or wheel through the Gamepad API. One driver a floor; everyone else sees the race on the rig's TV (the driver's page sends a compact frame ten times a second, the office passes it on, each page draws it) and can press **E** to watch up close. Esc or ✕ gets you out of the seat; leaving the floor or the office frees it. The building's fastest races and laps are kept in the office's `.agent-office/rig.json` and shown on the TV when nobody's racing. The office only takes a result whose laps could have been driven (none quicker than a lap flat out on the boost) and no quicker than its own clock saw since the green.

- Own files: `src/shared/racing.ts` (the track, the race, the CPU cars, the autopilot), `src/shared/rig.ts` (where the rig stands, its seat, frames, results and tables), `src/server/rig.ts` (one driver a floor, relaying, checking results, the tables), `src/client/world/rig.ts` (the model), `src/client/ui/rig.ts` (sitting, driving, watching, the TV), `src/client/ui/rigscreen.ts` (drawing the race), `src/client/features/rig/index.ts` (the `rig` kind: `sitInRig`, E at the rig, its hint and reach, updated each frame, your `wheel`, no first-person hands while zoomed, its `engine`), `src/client/state/slices/rig.ts` (`store.rig`/`store.rigFrame`, from the floor view and the `rig`/`rig.frame` messages), `src/client/sound/rig.ts` (the TV's beeps, fanfare and knocks); `tests/rig.test.ts`.
- Server: `rigs`/`rigChanged` on the `Fork` context (`src/server/fork/office.ts`); the `rig.*` handlers, `rigLeft` in `forkHooks` (leaving a floor or the office) and `rigView` in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`; the messages in `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`.

### Streams on the office TV

The lounge TV plays streams by itself too: **E** at the TV opens its window, where anyone on the floor, guests too, pastes a YouTube link (a video, a live one, shorts, with its `t=`) or a Twitch one (a channel, live; a past broadcast, from its `t=`). Everyone on that floor sees it on the TV, from the same moment (a video is put back in step when it drifts more than 3 s; a live channel just plays live), louder the closer they stand (music and master volume, silent from 30 m or off the floor). **Watch full screen** opens it big, with the player's own controls (pausing it there is yours; closing puts it back in step). **Stop stream** turns it off. Screen sharing is as it was, and goes first: while someone shares, the TV shows their screen and the stream waits, carrying on from where everyone is once they stop. The window also offers **Watch <name>'s screen** and **Share your screen**. Each floor keeps what's on in `.agent-office/tv.json`; the toast says "📺 <who> put on <title>".

How it gets onto the TV: a browser can't draw another site's player into WebGL, so the player is a real iframe laid over the page and bent each frame with a CSS `matrix3d` onto where the TV's screen is (the projective transform of its four corners, `client/tvquad.ts`). It shows while you're in the room in front of the TV, within 26 m, with the screen in view and no wall or other box between (a line against the floor's colliders, a few times a second); `pointer-events: none`, so it never steals mouse-look. Otherwise the TV shows a card with the title and how it's going ("click anywhere to start it", "can't play here: …", "it has ended"). People walking in front of the TV don't hide it (it's on top of the scene). Twitch's player gets `parent=<this page's host>`; YouTube's the `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` referrer. Both start muted and are turned up to your volume.

- `src/shared/embeds.ts`: what the DJ's and the TV's links share (plain web link check, `t=`, YouTube's one video). `src/shared/tv.ts`: reading a TV link (only YouTube and Twitch, by exact host; clips refused), `tests/tv.test.ts`.
- `src/server/embeds.ts`: `LinkPlayer` (what's on, saved, its title from oEmbed, not too often per person), shared with the DJ booth. `src/server/tv.ts`: `OfficeTv` (tv.json) and `tvMessage` (tv.play/tv.stop: on a floor of the office map, rate limited, told to the floor).
- `src/client/embeds.ts`: `EmbedPlayer` (keeping a site's player in step on the office clock, its volume, blocked/failed/ended/held) and YouTube's player, shared with the DJ sets. `src/client/tv.ts`: `TvStreams` (Twitch's player, laying the iframe over the TV or the big player, the TV's card), `src/client/tvquad.ts` (the matrix and the line of sight), `src/client/ui/tv.ts` (the TV's window and the big player).
- The DJ sets now use these shared files (`src/shared/djset.ts`, `src/server/djset.ts`, `src/client/djset.ts` keep only their own parts); they behave as before, except that a set whose title arrived before you went up no longer gets stuck loading.
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts` (fork file): `tv.play`/`tv.stop` (ClientMsg), `tv` (ServerMsg); `src/shared/protocol/floors.ts`: `FloorView.tv`.
  - `src/server/floor.ts`: `Floor.tv` (an `OfficeTv` in the floor's data folder).
  - `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts`: the floor view carries `tv` (`tv: tvView`); the `tv.play`/`tv.stop` handlers and `tvView` are in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`.
  - `src/server/guests.ts`: `tv.play`, `tv.stop` in `GUEST`.
  - `src/client/sound/index.ts`: `tvVolume()` (`sound/screens.ts`); `src/client/sound/core.ts`: `masterGain()`.
  - `src/client/features/tv/index.ts`: `tvStreams`/`tvPicture()` (the TV's picture: a share, the stream's card, or idle), the `tv` message and `msg.tv` on arrival, `setOn`/`frame` after the scene's drawn (the `render` tick), E at the TV opens `showTv()`, `watchBig`, the TV's hint, the idle screen's words, `voiceChanged`.

### Games on the roof

Four table games on the rooftop bar: a pool table (eight-ball, simplified) and a kicker on the open deck west of the dance floor, south of the darts and axes, and an air hockey table and a table tennis table side by side in the middle of the deck, north of the sun loungers. **E** at a table steps up to it: the camera glides to your end and the mouse plays (✕ or Esc steps back). The first two up play each other, someone alone plays the computer, and with both sides taken **E** watches. Guests play too.

The first player's page (the table's host) runs the game, the computer included, and sends a snapshot of the table about 25 times a second through the office to everyone on the roof; the other player's page sends its moves to the host the same way. The office only checks who may send what (only the host snapshots, only the other player moves, only on the roof, a rate limit each, plain numbers only), relays to the roof, and keeps who's at which table, the score and the last snapshot for whoever comes up later. When the host leaves (steps back, goes downstairs, closes the page), the other player runs the table against the computer; somebody new at a table starts a new game.

- Own files:
  - `src/shared/tablegames/`: the games, pure and shared: `tables.ts` (where the tables stand, the wire types, the checks), `game.ts` (what a game is), `hockey.ts`, `pingpong.ts`, `kicker.ts`, `pool.ts` (physics, rules, the computer), `index.ts`.
  - `src/server/tablegames.ts`: the seats (`RoofTables`: join, leave, host handover, snapshot and move checks) and `tableMessage`.
  - `src/client/tablegames/models.ts` (the four tables in 3D, and what's on them), `play.ts` (stepping up, running and drawing the games, the computer, the camera, the bar at the top, sounds), `tablegames.css`.
  - `src/client/features/tablegames/index.ts`: the `table` kind (E at a table, its hint and reach), `tables.update` each frame, no hands while the camera's at a table. `src/client/sound/tablegames.ts`: `tableGame()`.
  - Server: `roofTables` and `toRoof` on the `Fork` context and in `roofExtras` (`src/server/fork/office.ts`); the four `table.*` handlers and `leftTable` (in `forkHooks`: off the roof, and on disconnect) in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`; the messages in `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`.
  - `tests/tablegames.test.ts` (physics and rules), `tests/tablegames-server.test.ts` (sessions and relaying).
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/shared/protocol/floors.ts`: `FloorView.tables`.
  - `src/server/office/views.ts`: `roofView` carries `tables` (`roofExtras`). `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts`: `tables: noView` in `views`.
  - `src/server/guests.ts`: the four `table.*` messages in `GUEST`.
  - `src/client/world/types.ts`: `Interactable.table`.

### Padel

Two padel courts in the padel hall (the building, the way in, the stand, the gallery and its café are the hall's own, see `src/shared/hall.ts`): blue courts with white lines, the net on its posts, glass back walls (3 m) with the wire fence on top to 4 m, side glass 2 m out from each back wall and fence between, an opening in each side on either side of the net, lights overhead, and a scoreboard hanging over each court's north end for the gallery (both faces). **E** at an opening opens a chooser (✕ top right): a place (south or north end, right or left; 1–4) or 👀 Watch. It's always two against two: the computer plays every place nobody has, so one person plays with a computer partner against two computer players, and two can play each other (a computer partner each) or together. Guests and party guests play too.

Playing, the camera glides in behind your player; they run for the ball by themselves (the chooser's "run for the ball" box; **WASD** steers while held), the mouse aims (a ring on the court), click or **Space** swings (the racket meets the ball as it comes past, and how well the swing's timed decides how hard and true it goes: early or late floats, strays, and now and then finds the net or their glass on the full), **Shift** lobs, a high ball near the net is smashed. ✕ or **Esc** steps off through the nearest opening, back to mouse-look.

Rules, simplified but recognisable: the serve is underhand after a bounce, diagonally into the box across (from the right when the game's points add up even), two tries; the ball must bounce once in the other half before it touches that half's walls; after the bounce it may come off the glass (any number of times) and be played, volleys are fine except on the return of serve; a second bounce, the wire fence, or out through an opening or over the top ends the point (for the hitter after the bounce, against them before it); into the net and down on your own side is the other side's. 15, 30, 40, game, a **golden point** at 40–40, first to **four games** (no sets or tie-break: four computer players take 5–8 minutes, `tests/padel.test.ts` checks it). The teams serve a game each in turn, the players within a team by turns. A new match starts by itself 8 s after one ends.

It runs like the table games on the roof: the first person on a court hosts it (their page runs the game and the computer players and sends a snapshot about 25 times a second through the office to everyone in the hall); the others on the court send their moves (how they move, a swing with its aim) to the host. The office checks who may send what (only inside the hall, only the court's host snapshots, only its other players move, a rate limit each, plain numbers of a bounded size), relays, and keeps who's on which place, the games and the last snapshot for whoever comes in later. When the host leaves (steps off, leaves the hall, closes the page) the next person on the court takes over from the last snapshot; someone joining mid-match takes a computer player's place and the match goes on; the last one off frees the court.

- Own files:
  - `src/shared/padel/`: `court.ts` (the court's frame, glass/fence/openings, slots and teams, the wire types and checks, `PadelClientMsg`/`PadelServerMsg`), `rules.ts` (scoring, the golden point, who serves and receives), `game.ts` (ball physics, the rules in play, the players' legs, swings and shots, the computer, encode/decode; pure).
  - `src/server/padel.ts`: the places (`PadelCourts`: join, leave, host handover, snapshot and move checks) and `padelMessage`.
  - `src/client/hall/courts.ts` (the courts in 3D, figures with rackets, the ball, scoreboards; **the hook for the hall**: `buildCourts(parent)` returns `{ group, colliders, interactables, views, update }`, and the hall adds the colliders and interactables to its own; `currentCourts()` hands them to the game), `src/client/hall/padel.ts` (`PadelPlay`: the chooser, hosting and drawing the courts, the camera, input, the bar at the top, sounds), `padel.css`.
  - `src/client/lab/padel.html` + `padel.ts`: a lab page for the courts and a computer rally (`view=play|watch|gallery|top`, `t=`), for headless screenshots with `lab/shot.mjs`.
  - `tests/padel.test.ts` (scoring, serving, the court's edges, the rules in play, physics, snapshots, four computer players play a match), `tests/padel-server.test.ts` (sessions, handover, checks, relaying).
- Server: `padelCourts` and `toHall` on the `Fork` context (`src/server/fork/office.ts`); the five `padel.*` handlers and `leftCourt` (in `forkHooks`: out of the hall, and on disconnect) in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`; `PadelClientMsg`/`PadelServerMsg` in `ForkClientMsg`/`ForkServerMsg` (`src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`).
- Client: `src/client/features/places/index.ts`: `padel` (a `PadelPlay`), the courts built into the hall, the `padel` kind (E at a court, its hint and reach), `padel.update` each frame, no hands while the camera's on a court, `hides` and `seesYou`. `src/client/sound/padel.ts`: `padel()` (racket, bounce, glass knock, fence rattle, net, chimes; quiet from far away).
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/server/guests.ts`: the five `padel.*` messages in `GUEST`. `src/server/party.ts`: `padel`, `padel.sync`, `padel.input` in `PARTY_SEES`.
  - `src/server/tablegames.ts`: `Bucket` exported (padel's rate limits).
  - `src/client/world/types.ts`: `Interactable.court`.
  - `src/client/sound/index.ts`: `padel()`.
  - `src/client/core/loop.ts`: your own body hidden while the camera's on a court (`seesYou`). `src/client/features/peers/index.ts`: the people on a court hidden in the hall (`hides`; the court draws them). `src/client/main.ts`: `__office.padel`.
  - `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`: words.

### TURN for voice from outside

Voice and screen sharing connect browsers directly (WebRTC). With only STUN that fails for people outside the Mac's network behind strict routers, office networks or mobile data: they are "in voice" and their mic lights up, but nothing arrives. With a Cloudflare TURN key the office fetches short-lived TURN credentials (48 h, refreshed every 12 h, retried after 5 min on failure) and hands them to every page in the welcome's `ice`, after the office's own STUN servers. Without a key nothing changes. Port-53 URLs are dropped (browsers block them).

- The key: `<office>/.agent-office/turn.json` = `{"keyId": "...", "apiToken": "..."}` (chmod 600), or the environment's `CF_TURN_KEY_ID` / `CF_TURN_API_TOKEN`. Read at start: restart the office after adding it.
- `src/server/turn.ts` (+ `tests/turn.test.ts`); made, started and stopped in `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`createFork`, `startFork`, `stopFork`, hooked into `src/server/server.ts`); the welcome's `ice` (`ctx.turn.servers()`) in `src/server/ws/connection.ts`.
- Cloudflare: first 1,000 GB a month free (shared with their SFU), then $0.05/GB; voice needs a few MB per person and hour.

### A doorbell

A ding-dong for everyone in the office when a person comes in, with a toast "🔔 Ada came in". Workers never ring (they aren't people in the office), and neither do you, the people already there when you arrive, a reload or a dropped connection coming back (gone less than 90 s), or a second tab of someone who's here. A crowd arriving at once rings once, but everyone is named. It plays on the effects volume.

- `src/client/doorbell.ts`: who's here and whether an arrival rings; `src/client/features/doorbell/index.ts`: the bell, on the `welcome`, `floor.enter` and `peer.join`/`peer.leave` messages; `src/client/sound/doorbell.ts`: the ding-dong; `tests/doorbell.test.ts`.
- Hooks in upstream files: `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`doorbell()`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installDoorbell(ctx)`).

### The front door from the street

Only the bottom floor has the exit door; on every other floor's street it stays shut (upstream, `world/office/ground.ts`). Back from the gym or the casino onto your own floor, walking up the steps to it went nowhere. Now coming up to it on the landing there lets you in: the lights dip and you're just inside the bottom floor's door, facing in.

- `src/client/features/frontdoor/index.ts` (a `world` tick: on a floor above the bottom one, on the landing at the door at that floor's street level → `travel.placeTrip` to the bottom floor, and on its `floor.enter` placed just inside); hook in `src/client/main.ts` (`installFrontDoor(ctx, core, parts)`).

### Bungee off the roof

A wooden jetty with railings runs from the rooftop bar's deck out over the street-side (south) edge, east of the sun loungers (x 10.25..11.75, through a gap in the glass railing and the planters), to a steel platform with a yellow-and-black edge 3.4 m past the facade. A yellow gantry over it holds the rope's anchor on an arm out past the edge, with a BUNGEE sign; a board at the jetty's start shows the day's jumps. **E** on the platform: the office puts you on the rope (one jumper at a time; "Someone's on the rope" otherwise, and a few seconds' breather after your own jump), the gate swings open, 3-2-1, and you dive off head first: free fall down past the facade, the rope pulls taut, two or three damped bounces, a moment hanging head down, the winch pulls you back up and you swing over onto the platform, 12–18 s in all. The fall is as deep as the building is tall (`roofDrop`), the rope sized so your head stops 2.2 m over the street, and the drop comes down between the plaza's two trees in front of the building. Your controls are off meanwhile (**F** or **Space** mid-fall does a salto); in first person you see it through your own eyes (straight down at the street, then upside down out at the city), in third the camera follows your body and never goes through the facade. Everyone on the roof sees the jumper fall and bounce on the same curve (worked out from when the office said the jump started) with the rope from the anchor to their ankles, and gets a toast "🪂 Ada jumped!". Wind rises while you fall and the rope twangs as it catches (synth, effects volume). Guests and party guests jump too.

**Seen from below**: the jetty is visible from every floor of the office, its balcony and the street, sticking out over the south edge at the top of the building (on the tower's top, `streetBelow(index) + roofDrop(count)` over your floor), and so is a jump off it: a stand-in figure in the jumper's color (and look, if they're about) with their name tag falls past the facade and bounces on the rope on the same curve, the rope twangs from up there, and everyone in the building gets the "🪂 Ada jumped!" toast. Not from the casino, gym, padel or soccer hall (their own rooms).

- Own files:
  - `src/shared/bungee.ts`: where the jetty and the anchor are, `BungeeState`, and the jump: `bungeePlan(drop)` (the rope's length and stiffness fitted so it never goes deeper than the margin, damping included; a fixed-step simulation of the fall and bounces, kept per height) and `bungeePose(drop, t)` (count, fall, hang, winch, climb, done), `bungeeDuration`, `bungeeDay`.
  - `src/server/bungee.ts`: `BungeeRope` (who's on the rope and since when, one at a time, the per-person cooldown, the day's jumps reset at local midnight; in memory) and `bungeeMessage` (only from the roof; the drop from the building's floors).
  - `src/client/world/bungee.ts`: the jetty, platform, railings, gantry, gate, signs, colliders and the interactable. `src/client/bungee.ts`: `Bungee` (asks for the rope, holds your player on the platform while you're on it, poses the jumper's body, draws the rope, flies your camera, the countdown, the toast, the sounds, the keys; from below, a second jetty of its own and the jumper's stand-in `Person`, lifted into your floor's frame by `below()`).
  - `src/client/features/bungee/index.ts`: `below` (the roof deck's height over your floor, or null on the roof or across the street), the `bungee` kind (E at the platform, its hint and reach), its messages, its keys (an `activity` key), `bungee.update` after everyone's moved (the `others` tick). `src/client/sound/bungee.ts`: `bungee()`, `BungeeWind`.
  - `tests/bungee.test.ts`.
- The protocol: `bungee.jump` (ClientMsg), `bungee {state}` (ServerMsg, to everyone in the building: the jump's seen from below), `FloorView.bungee` in every view (the roof's and each floor's) for arrivals.
- Server: `bungeeRope` on the `Fork` context (`src/server/fork/office.ts`); the `bungee.jump` handler (`toBuilding` is `broadcast`), `offRope` (broadcasts; in `forkHooks`: leaving the roof, and on disconnect) and `bungeeView` in `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`; the two messages in `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`.
- Hooks in upstream files:
  - `src/shared/protocol/floors.ts`: `FloorView.bungee`.
  - `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts`: `bungee: bungeeView` in `views` (every floor's view and the roof's).
  - `src/server/guests.ts`: `bungee.jump` in `GUEST`. `src/server/party.ts`: `bungee` in `PARTY_SEES` and `VIEW_AS_IS`.
  - `src/client/features/rooftop/world.ts`: the south edge's railing and the planters there leave a gap for the jetty, `buildBungeeJetty()` added to the roof, `Rooftop.bungee`.
  - `src/client/sound/index.ts`: `bungee()` and `bungeeWind()`.
  - `src/client/main.ts`: `installBungee(...)`, `__office.bungee`; `src/client/core/parts.ts`: `bungee`.
  - `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`: words.

### Der Brecher (the roller coaster)

DER BRECHER is a steel coaster wound round the office tower itself. Its **station** hangs off the roof's north edge beside the DJ's stage, at deck level: through a gap in the railing (under a pink arch) onto a steel platform with a fence and gates along the track, a canopy over platform and train with the name in lit letters on top (both ways: to the deck and out over the city), a board over the track (*Einsteigen!*, *Abfahrt in 7*, the speed while it's out) and, at the platform's west end, the ride photo's monitor over the leaderboard. **E** on the platform gets you in (any free seat, the front first); a few seconds (`COUNTDOWN_MS`, 9 s) after the first one's in it goes, and others can hop in meanwhile (**E** again gets you out before it goes). Four cars of two seats, hot pink with a yellow stripe, lap bars that swing down for the last seconds and up again back in the station. Guests and party guests ride too.

**The way round** (`shared/coaster-route.ts`), heading east out of the station: the chain lift climbs over the north-east corner (above any floor's back office) and up the east side, along the top to the south-east corner and round over the street side and the FLOGGE OFFICE letters; then the **first drop, straight down the facade** past every storey's fins, windows and balconies, a couple of metres west of the bungee rope (a jumper falls beside the train); the pull-out along the plaza's edge, where the ride photo's camera on its own slim post by the U-turn **flashes**; a U-turn over the road, a short launch and the **loop over the street** (riders upside down over the traffic); left over the sidewalk and in through the ground floor's south wall into a **glass tube under its ceiling**, north past the stairs, along the desks and out through the north wall; **straight up the vertical lift** behind the building (turning a quarter as it climbs, so it tips over the top heading east), the brake run and home. Seen from above it's the same for any height (the minimap draws it once); the taller the building, the longer the drop and the vertical lift, and on a low one the lift hill climbs higher so the drop is still straight down.

**The track** (`shared/coaster-track.ts`, `coasterTrack(storeys)`, kept per height): the route's points a metre apart through a centripetal Catmull-Rom spline, resampled every 25 cm; a parallel-transport frame (turned where the route says, eased upright where the track's neither steep nor upside down) banked about the heartline toward what the riders feel (gravity and the turn), smoothed. **The ride**: the chain and the station's tires at their speed, energy kept going down and lost going up, rolling friction and drag, magnetic trims and brakes where the speed's too much (on the drop of a tall building, before the U-turn and the tube), an LSM launch into the loop. A train's place is a pure function of the track and the time since it went (`sAtTime`), so every page sees it in the same spot; the cars follow one by one by their own places along it. 47 s on a 2-storey building, 60 s on 8, 67 s on 20. There's no top to the height: on a tall tower the vertical lift runs faster (up to 9 m/s, letting go a few metres short of the top so the train slows by itself for the slow chain over it) and the drop's trims hold it at 16.8 m/s. **A floor added or taken off while the train's out** would move the roof, the station, the drop and the tube under it: the office checks the height every 200 ms and whenever the floors change, and brings the train straight back into the station (a ride for everyone in it, `CoasterState.halted`, a toast), laid for the new height; till it says so, a page that already has the new height shows the train waiting in the station, so nobody sees it run through the building. `tests/coaster.test.ts` checks for 1 to 20 storeys: closed and smooth (never tighter than 2.5 m), riders between −1.5 g and 5 g (−1.2 to 4.4 in fact), under 2 g sideways and 1.6 g along, never stalling; and the train's envelope (rails, spine, car sides, heads, hands up) clear of the tower (bar the tube's portals), every storey's balconies, the facade's landmarks (the CREATE blade, the letters, the bulb and its arm, the paper plane, the pencil), the bungee jetty and a jumper's fall (a metre to spare), the trees and lamps, the side streets, the bowling centre's block, the halls across the street and the golf, the pool on the roof with its palms, slide and diving tower, the sky bar and the stage; 5.5 m and more over the road wherever it crosses; and in the ground floor the tube under the ceiling (`tests/coaster-tube.test.ts`: every rider's actual figure, the same Person the page draws, sat in every seat with hands in the lap or up and waving, keeps 0.25 m to the glass and 0.3 m to the ceiling and the edges of the walls' holes; the glass and the rings round it where it goes through the walls fit their holes and stay under the ceiling; the tube's 1.58 m round, its rails 4.17 m up, `TUBE_*` in `shared/coaster.ts`), clear of the speakers, the cone lamps, the fire pole and its hole, the ladder, the loft, the elevator's shaft, heads on the stairs and what's on the walls. **Every interior goes round the tube** (`world/office/interior/clear.ts`, on the ground floor only): one pass over what an interior put up under the ceiling and its lamps, against the tube's keep-out (`tubeBoxes()` in `shared/coaster-keepout.ts`, a box per half metre of it): something long running across it (a beam, a duct, a light line, a moulding, a strip along the wall) is cut where the tube goes through and carries on either side, anything else it would run into (a hanging plant, a lamp, a vent) isn't put up; the tests build all eight interiors' decor and lamps and check nothing's left in the tube.

**Supports** (`shared/coaster-supports.ts`, `coasterSupports(track)`), calm rather than a forest of towers, all of them round steel tubes painted the spine's white: **up along the building** (the station, the brake run, the lift hill, the crest round the south-east corner, the top of the drop) **brackets off the top storey**, whatever storey that is: each a pair of struts from a steel plate on the top storey's wall, under the roof's slab and over its windows, up and out to the track's spine, every 6 m or so, off the wall the track's going along (round a corner, the other wall; slid along the wall past what's in the way, like the bungee's jetty); never on a window, a balcony door or a fin, and every strut clear of the building, every storey's balconies, the facade's landmarks (the letters, the CREATE blade, the bulb and its arm, the pencil, the paper plane), the bungee and the roof's things (`bracketKeepouts(storeys)`). On a low building (one or two storeys) the lift hill climbs high over the roof for the drop, so the brackets there are long raking struts. Where the track runs straight up or down the facade (the drop, the vertical lift) a tie back to the wall at every storey's slab it passes, level to the spine where the track is there; where the spine's round the back of the vertical lift (the riders face the wall), a yoke: a bar across behind the spine, tied back to the wall beside the train. **Down on the street** plain supports straight under the track: a couple of single round columns on the plaza's edge, each with a Y head under the spine, on a round concrete footing. Where the track's over the road (the U-turn, the launch, the loop's way out), where nothing can stand under it, a plain portal: a post on the plaza, one past the far sidewalk, and one beam between them right under the track, at least 5.5 m over the road; three of them, nothing else (no gate, no frame, no rungs). The ride photo's camera has a slim post of its own on the plaza's edge (`photoMount(track)`: the post, the camera on top aimed at the train, the moment it fires); the flash goes off on it and the photo's taken from just in front of its lens (`shot`), with the camera itself hidden for the picture, so nothing of it is in the photo (`tests/coaster-supports.test.ts` checks every bit of it is behind where the picture's taken). Never on a sidewalk, never in the road, nothing inside the ground floor (the tube hangs under its ceiling). `tests/coaster.test.ts` checks every column against the keep-outs; `tests/coaster-supports.test.ts`, for 1 to 20 storeys, that every bracket's on the top storey's wall clear of its openings and fins, every strut clear of the building and the keep-outs and meeting the track, that nothing by the building stands taller than 13 m off the street or up past the roof, and that every piece carries track: every strut, tie and bracket reaches the spine (a yoke's arm holds the bar that does), every column holds the track up or carries a beam, a portal has one beam, level, over the road high enough and clear of the track (`inTrack`); and the photo camera's post off the road and the sidewalks, clear of the keep-outs and the track, the camera on top of it. The fins' places are shared (`shared/facade-fins.ts`, `finXs`/`storeyFins`/`storeyHoles`), so the facade draws them where the brackets know they are.

**Through the ground floor, for its riders** (`client/coaster/tunnel.ts`): riders are up on the roof as far as the office goes (the station's there), and up there only the roof is in the scene. But the office's own world is still there, hidden, laid out as the bottom floor's (`syncStack` puts it at index 0 on the roof). So while a rider's eyes are in the tube it's shown, lowered to where the ground floor is under the roof, painted and furnished the way the ground floor is, with the ground floor's workers typing at their desks below (`CoasterState.typists`: name, color and desk of each, as the train went; party guests see workers by name at their desks anyway); out of the tube it's hidden again and painted the way the roof left it. From the ground floor itself you see the tube under your ceiling and the train rush through it.

**Riding** (`client/coaster/ride.ts`): your body waits on the platform, held there (so a reload or a reconnect finds you on the roof), and your eyes are in your seat: free to look round with the mouse, a rumble through the seat, pressed down in the dips and lifted on the hills, the view widening as it picks up speed, the flash in your face at the photo. **H** (or **Space**) hands up, for everyone to see. Off only back in the station; leaving the roof or the office gives your seat up (`gone`). Sounds (`sound/coaster.ts`, synthesized, effects volume): the wind and the rails' rumble with the speed (heard from the train by everyone else), the chains' clack, screams from the cars on the drop, in the loop and into the tube (a voice each, pitched by who it is), the flash, the station's bell, the brakes, the lap bars, the countdown.

**The ride photo** (`client/coaster/photo.ts`): every page up on the roof draws the moment from the camera as the train passes (everyone in it, hands up or not: the same on every page) and puts it on the station's monitor; **E** at the monitor or the leaderboard under it opens it big to save, with the leaderboard. In first person what's under the crosshair counts: the platform, the train and the totem's screens carry what they're for. **Records**: the office keeps everyone's rides and their longest hands-up ride (`<dataDir>/coaster.json`, 0600, by account or name); the leaderboard at the station shows them and the rides so far. **At night** the name and the arch glow, the canopy's lights come on, and chase lights race round both rails in the rainbow.

- Own files:
  - `src/shared/coaster.ts` (the station, the train and its seats, `CoasterState`, the messages, `TUBE_PORTALS`), `coaster-route.ts` (the way round, `coasterRoute`, its zones and marks), `coaster-track.ts` (`coasterTrack`, `sAtTime`, `poseAt`, `rideDuration`), `coaster-supports.ts` (`coasterSupports`), `coaster-keepout.ts` (what it keeps clear of, for the supports and the tests).
  - `src/server/coaster.ts` (`Coaster`: seats, countdown, dispatch, hands up, back in the station; `CoasterRecords`; `coasterMessage`), `src/server/ws/handlers/coaster.ts` (`coasterHandlers`, `coasterHooks`, `coasterView`).
  - `src/client/coaster/` (`ride.ts`, `riders.ts`, `tunnel.ts`, `photo.ts`), `src/client/world/coaster/` (`track.ts` the rails, spine, ties, catwalk, chain, fins, chase lights and the glass tube; `sweep.ts`; `supports.ts`; `train.ts`; `station.ts`), `src/client/features/coaster/index.ts` (`installCoaster`: the `coaster` and `coasterphoto` kinds, the activity, the field of view, the tick), `src/client/sound/coaster.ts`.
  - `tests/coaster.test.ts`, `tests/coaster-server.test.ts`.
- The protocol: `coaster.board`, `coaster.leave`, `coaster.hands` (ClientMsg), `coaster {state}` (ServerMsg, to everyone in the building: it's seen from every floor), `FloorView.coaster` in every view.
- Hooks in upstream (and other fork) files:
  - `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`, `src/shared/protocol/floors.ts` (`FloorView.coaster`).
  - `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`coaster` on the Fork context: the data folder, the building's height, the ground floor's workers; stopped with the rest), `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts` (`coasterHandlers`, `coasterHooks` in `forkHooks`), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`coaster: coasterView`).
  - `src/server/guests.ts` (the three messages in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (`coaster` in `PARTY_SEES` and `VIEW_AS_IS`).
  - `src/client/features/rooftop/world.ts` (the gap in the north railing, `COASTER_GAP`).
  - The tube's holes in the ground floor's walls (`tubePortals(index)`): `src/client/world/office/storey-walls.ts`, `src/client/world/office/interior/index.ts` (the wall finish), `src/client/world/tower.ts` (the outside, from other floors and the roof), `src/client/world/facade/index.ts` (no fin or mural over them).
  - `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`coaster`, `setCoaster`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installCoaster`, `__office.coaster`), `src/client/core/parts.ts` (`coaster`).
  - `src/client/features/minimap/atlas.ts` (the track, dashed through the building), `pois.ts` (🎢 the station).
  - `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`; `tests/server-dispatch.test.ts` (the welcome's last field).
  - `src/client/world/office/interior/index.ts` (`clearOf(..., tubeBoxes())` on the ground floor), `src/server/office/floors.ts` (`floorsChanged` asks the coaster to check the height), `tests/support/css-loader.mjs` (a `?url` asset import is its path, for tests that build client meshes).

### The soccer hall

Across the street between the golf hole and the padel hall (x 2.5..21, z 36..72, where the last neighbour across the street stood; ≥ 1 m from the green and its fringe, 1 m from the padel hall), an indoor small-field soccer hall: a long dark green hall with a band of milky polycarbonate glazing under the eaves (lit at night), white SOCCER ARENA letters with a ball, glass doors under a steel canopy (a green light strip along its edge, downlights under it) on the north (street) face, the club's red and blue FLOGGE FC banners, uplights washing the facade at night, a match poster by the doors, a little practice goal on the lawn, and the SCENIC LOOP billboard, which moved up onto its roof. **E** at the doors goes in; **E** at the doors inside comes back out onto the street of the floor you came from. Inside is a place of its own like the casino and the padel hall (`SOCCER = '@soccer'` is your `floor` in there; interior coordinates, the pitch centred on 0): a 27 × 14 m pitch of artificial turf with mown stripes and crisp white lines (halfway line, centre circle, futsal-style penalty areas and spots, corner arcs) and the FLOGGE FC crest in the centre circle, 1 m boards all round (a rubber kick plate, LED panels on both faces, a padded top) with safety nets above (4.5 m behind the goals, 3 m along the sides), two 3 × 2 m goals (round white posts and bar, a frame back to a weighted base, nets that sag and billow out when the ball goes in), dugouts (red north, blue south of the halfway line) with curved acrylic roofs, bucket seats, bottle crates, cones and a ball bag along the west wall, the players' tunnel (SPIELERTUNNEL) between them and a locker room (KABINE) behind glass further north, a three-row stand behind each goal (blue end south, red end north west of the doors) and a gallery over the east walkway, padded walls with ribbed cladding and a band of windows (daylight, or the night outside), steel trusses with red and blue banners and rows of floodlights under the roof, and a big scoreboard over each end (RED n : m BLUE, the clock, what's going on; it flashes the scoring team's colour for a goal).

The hall's atmosphere (`world/soccer/look.ts`, the rules in `matchday.ts`): the stands fill with instanced fans (178 seats, red at the north end and the gallery's north half, blue at the south, neutral between; more of them with more people in the hall, always a good crowd during a match, nearly empty when nobody's in). They bob and chat, clap a kick-off and along with the chants, get half up with their hands up for a near miss (off the post or the bar, or hard into the end boards just wide), and jump with arms and scarves up when their team scores (the other end sits it out). The LED boards roll through friendly fake ads (Kultur am Regen, Bus kommt gleich, Bibelstunde Cocktails, Signal & Stille, Casino gegenüber, Padel Hall, …) and strobe GOAL! in the scorers' colour for a goal. The stadium announcer calls out the kick-off (ANPFIFF!), goals (⚽ TOOOR! Ann trifft – Rot 2:1 Blau, own goals too) and the final whistle in a big callout with an air horn. The floodlights flare as a match starts and dim a little between matches; at night the windows go dark and their pools on the pitch show more. Sounds (effects volume, only in the hall): a crowd murmur that swells with the crowd and the play, the roar of a goal, the "oooh", applause, rhythmic clapping now and then in play. All of it costs well under a millisecond a frame (one draw call per body part for the whole crowd, one mesh for every LED panel, the hall's plain parts merged into one).

Playing: **E** at the boards on the halfway line (either side) puts you in the team with fewer players (on a tie the one behind, else red; five a side at most), on the pitch in your half, with a bib and a ring under your feet in the team's colour for everyone to see; **E** there again leaves the pitch (so do leaving the hall and disconnecting; a reconnect joins again). The ball is the office's: `server/soccer/` runs the physics (`shared/soccer-ball.ts`) in fixed 1/60 s steps (`SIM_DT`, 30 ticks a second), tuned like a heavy futsal ball: rolling friction 1.5 m/s² plus drag 0.4/s (a 6 m/s pass dies after ~6 m, 7 m/s ~7.6 m), a low bounce (0.32; dropped from 2 m it comes up ~0.2 m, and the turf takes 15% of its pace on each bounce), air drag, the boards (restitution 0.62; the nets above them hold a high ball in, so it never leaves the pitch), the posts and the bar (0.55), the net nearly dead, and a ball lying dead against the boards or in a corner rolls gently back out. A goal is the whole ball over the line between the posts under the bar. Players' positions are their ordinary `move` messages; their speed comes from the last two, the way they face from `rotY`. Close control (`touchBall`): run onto the ball (its middle within 1 m of yours, low, slower than 15 m/s relative to you) and it's yours; the first touch cushions it (80% of its speed relative to you taken off). While you have it, it's kept on a soft spring 0.75 m ahead of your feet the way you run (a little lag on a turn; at a sprint up to 1.35 m, the knock-on), and standing it's trapped 0.55 m in front of you; you keep it within 1.8 m. An opponent clearly better placed (nearer, running at it) takes it off you; a close call pops it loose sideways, and whoever lost it waits 350 ms. A ball too fast or too high to take comes off players' bodies (they're 1.8 m tall, so a lob goes over). Kicking (`client/soccer/controls.ts`, `passKick`, `shotKick`): a tap of the left mouse button or Space passes (the teammate best in line with where you look, within ±29° and 1.5–22 m, led if they run, paced to arrive at ~3 m/s; with nobody there, ~8 m along your aim); holding it charges a shot (full after 0.9 s, at least 30% power) that goes along the crosshair, rising as far as you look up (up to 0.45 rad; first person); the right button (or C) does the same through the air (a lob that comes down just short of the teammate, or a chip). A kick let go of up to 0.2 s before the ball's in reach (1.5 m) waits for it, and one up to 0.2 s after it was still goes. On the page, the aim shows as a ring round the crosshair with the power meter under it, the receiver's name, and a line on the floor from the ball the way it'll go. The office takes a kick (`soccer.kick {power, dir, loft, lift}`) only from a player whose team may touch the ball, once per 250 ms, when the ball was within 1.5 + 0.35 m of where the office has them at some moment in the last 350 ms (or they're dribbling it), all numbers clamped. The office sends the ball about 15 times a second while it moves (at once after a kick, a hit or a change of who has it), with its physics step `k` and who has it `c`. Your own kick goes at once on your page (the same `kick` and the same steps as the office, so the two agree to the last digit; once the office has had the kick its snapshots, run on by the round trip, nudge it), and so does taking and dribbling the ball (it stays at your feet as you run; the office taking it off you ends that). Everyone else's ball is played back 100 ms behind the office's clock, on a curve between the snapshots either side, run on for up to 250 ms when one's late; switching between the two melts away over a few frames. The match (`server/soccer/match.ts`, on its own clock for the tests): it starts once both teams have a player; a 2 s kick-off freeze with the ball on the centre spot, then the kicking-off team touches it first (for up to 3 s); 5 minutes of playing time (the clock stops for goals and kick-offs) or first to 5 goals; after a goal a 3 s celebration (toast "⚽ GOAL! Red (Ann) · Red 1:0 Blue", confetti in the goal, the cheer and the whistle), then the team that conceded kicks off; the final whistle, the result as a toast, 10 s, a new match. A team left empty pauses it (clock stopped, score kept) and it kicks off again once both have players; nobody left on the pitch resets it. Meanwhile (waiting or paused) the ball's free to kick about, and practice goals just bring it back to the centre. Guests and party guests play too.

Stats, replays and the kit: the match keeps its statistics (`server/soccer/stats.ts`, `MatchStats`, the match's `stats`): every touch in play (a kick, or running into the ball) in order, by who someone is (`account:<id>` or `name:<name>`, like the casino's chips). A goal gets the scorer (the last touch, if it's their team's; else an own goal, the toucher's), the assist (the last touch before the scorer's by someone else, if it's a teammate's and at most `ASSIST_MS` = 6 s before the goal) and the minute; a kick is a shot when it's hard enough, from near enough, and the physics run on (`runOn`) takes it to the other goal's end line near the posts, on target when it'd go in (a goal always counts as one); a pass is completed when a kick's next touch is a teammate's; possession is the playing time each team had the last touch; a save is a shot on target whose next touch is a defender's near their own goal line. At the final whistle the man of the match (`shared/soccer-stats.ts` `pickMvp`: goals ×3, assists ×2, shots on target and saves ×1; ties to more goals, then the winners) and everyone's match go on the all-time leaderboard (`server/soccer/records.ts`, `SoccerRecords`: matches, wins, goals, assists, MVP awards, and the shirt number they keep, 1–99, from their key's hash or the next free one; `<dataDir>/soccer.json`, 0600, written beside it and moved over it). The `soccer` state carries `stats` and `leaders` (the top five) and each player's `number`; no new messages. The scoreboards (`client/soccer/scoreboard.ts`, drawn into the interior's scoreboard canvas) show RED n : m BLUE, the clock, the phase, the scorers under each team ("⚽ Ann 3'"), shots and a possession bar, and at full time the result, the MVP and the two teams side by side; a Hall of Fame board on the north wall west of the scoreboard shows the top five. On the page (`client/soccer/show.ts`, `SoccerShow`, made by `SoccerPlace`): a line under the score bar (the last goal, Tab), the stats panel on **Tab** (the match, the players, the leaderboard; ✕, Tab or Esc closes it), the kit (`client/soccer/kit.ts`: a team-coloured shirt over body and arms with the short name and number on the back and the number on the chest, shorts and socks; the ring under the feet stays `SoccerPlace`'s) and the moves laid over each body after `Person.update` (a kick's leg swing from the ball's `by`, the scorer's celebration, `pickCelebration`: arms up, knee slide, aeroplane or jumping, teammates within 4.5 m put a hand up, and a dive, `diveSide`, for someone in their own penalty area when a fast ball will pass them 0.4–2.2 m to the side). The instant replay (`client/soccer/replay.ts`): each page records what it shows (the ball, and where everyone on the pitch stood and faced, 7 s back, and kicks); `REPLAY.startAfterMs` (1.8 s) after a goal it cuts the 3.2 s before it and 0.2 s after and plays it back on everyone's screen from a TV camera swinging round the ball (letterbox bars, a REPLAY badge, the last 0.8 s at half speed, then SLOW-MO), putting the bodies and the ball where they were; **Space** or **Esc** skips it, and a kickoff ends it. `GOAL_MS` is 6.5 s so the celebration and the replay fit before the kickoff (a test checks).

Slide tackles and fouls (`shared/soccer-tackle.ts` the rules, `server/soccer/tackle.ts` the office's side, `client/soccer/tackle.ts` the page's): **Q** (or **Ctrl**; while you play, Q's own jobs, putting back a card or dropping the basketball, don't apply on the pitch, and a bare Ctrl does nothing else in the 3D view) slides you the way you face. The slide's curve is the same everywhere (`SLIDE`: 3.5 m in 450 ms on an ease-out with exponent 1.6, so it leaves at ~12 m/s and stops dead; then 400 ms lying and 400 ms getting up, `SLIDE_MS` 1250; it stops `SLIDE_EDGE` 0.4 m inside the boards: `makeSlide`, `slideAt`). The page sends `soccer.slide {dir, x, z}` and at once drives you along the curve itself (`player.rig`, so its `move`s are where the office has you; keys and kicks do nothing until you're up; the view sinks up to 0.95 m in first person, 0.55 m in third, `player.eyeDrop`). The office checks it (on a team, on the pitch, one per `cooldownMs` 2 s, not sliding already, not at a set piece, a goal or full time, nor in the other team's kick-off: `slideRefused`; it takes the page's start when it's within `SLIDE_TRUST` 1.5 m of where it has you), rounds the curve to the millimetre and sends it to everyone (`soccer.slide {id, x, z, dir, d}`; a refusal only to you, at most every 600 ms, and your page stands you up again). Every page moves that body along the same curve and poses it (`Person.rig()`, after the kit's moves: down on the hip leaning back, the leading leg out, a hand behind) with turf flying from the boot. Every tick, over the slide's first `activeMs` 350 ms, the office looks for contacts on a fixed 1/240 s grid (`firstContact`): the ball (its middle within `BALL_HIT_R` 0.5 m of a foot zone `FOOT_AHEAD` 0.75 m ahead of the body, under 0.5 m high, and only while the slider's team may touch it) or an opponent's legs (the sliding leg, a segment `LEG_LEN` 1 m out along the slide, within `LEGS_R` 0.4 m of where they stand). Whichever comes first counts; both in the same instant is the ball's. The ball: poked on along the slide (`POKE_V` 7 m/s, or the slide's speed + 1.5), or, when the slide is slower than `WIN_V` 5 m/s by then, rolled on to stop 0.6 m past where the slider ends up (`pokeOf`, won); whoever had it, or stood within 1.8 m of it, can neither take nor block it for 600 ms, and an opponent's ball is a tackle won (`tackle` event, a short cheer). The legs, in a match in play (no fouls in practice): a foul (`foul` event: who, on whom, penalty, card), the match goes to `freekick` or `penalty` (`SoccerMatch.foul`, the clock stopped), the ball dead still at `foulSpot` (where the fouled player stood, kept `SPOT_EDGE` 2 m off the boards), or on the penalty spot (6 m out) when the foul was in the fouler's own penalty area (`inPenaltyArea`: within 5 m of the goal line between the posts). From `SETUP_MS` 0.8 s on, the fouled team's first kick (`setPieceKick`; at a penalty only the taker's, the fouled player) restarts play (`restart`); nobody runs the ball along meanwhile, and after `SET_PIECE_MS` 8 s it goes on by itself ("⏱️ … not taken: play on"). The pages keep the fouling team `FREEKICK_RING` 3 m off the ball for `RING_MS` 3 s, and at a penalty everyone but the taker and the keeper `PENALTY_RING` 4 m (`ringPush`; a yellow ring on the floor shows it); they put the taker `TAKER_BACK` 1.4 m behind the spot and the keeper (the defenders' player nearest their goal, picked by the office) on the line. The fouled player goes down for `FALL_MS` 1.5 s (on their own page they can't move meanwhile). Fouls count per player per match (`MatchStats.foul`): the second is a yellow card, the third a red one (`cardFor`), which sends the player off the pitch and keeps them off for `RED_OUT_MS` 60 s. `SoccerPlayer.card` carries it: 🟨/🟥 by the name in the **Tab** stats, the bookings on the scoreboards (drawn cards: not every canvas has colour emoji). Stats: tackles won, fouls and fouls suffered per player, tackles and fouls per team (in Tab, and two more rows at full time); the man of the match gets a point per tackle won (`mvpScore`). The atmosphere: the referee's double whistle (`sound.soccer('foul')`), the stands whistle and boo (crowd act `boo`, `soccerCrowd('boo')`), the announcer calls FOUL! (FREISTOSS für Rot) or ELFMETER!, and 1.7 s later the card (🟨 Gelbe Karte – Bob, `cardCallout`); a clean tackle gets applause and a short cheer from the tackler's end.

- Own files:
  - Tackles: `src/shared/soccer-tackle.ts` (the slide's curve, contacts, pokes, the set pieces' places, cards, `slideRefused`), `src/server/soccer/tackle.ts` (`SoccerTackles`: slides, contacts each tick, fouls, red cards' bans), `src/client/soccer/tackle.ts` (`SoccerTackle`: Q and Ctrl, driving your slide, everyone's bodies and poses, the spray, falls, the set pieces' ring and places), `tests/soccer-tackle.test.ts`.

### One city round the office

The city the rooftop bar used to look out over on its own is the office's now: one city, laid out once (`src/shared/city.ts`), standing down on the street round the building, the same from every floor's windows, the balcony, the street and the roof. Streets run every 56 m (the one in front of the office is one of them) with sidewalks, curbs, crossings with zebras and bollards, blocks of buildings (lower close by, so the roof still looks out over them, a skyline of towers off to the north-east), parks, street lamps, street trees, benches, bins, bike stands, bus stops and hydrants, and cars of their own that drive up and down the streets, turn round at the end and stop for anyone in the road (you, others on foot, the garage's cars). The buildings you can walk to have shops on their ground floor where they face a street (a bakery, a café, a pizzeria, a pharmacy, a florist, a bookshop, a kiosk, a bar, a Späti, a hairdresser): shop windows with goods on shelves, a door, a striped awning and a sign, the windows lit from inside at night. The farm, the sea, the creek and the scenic loop stay country: south of the office's street the town is two rows deep inside the loop, and no city street meets the loop out in the country (only the office's own street does), so its edges still carry a car round it hands off. The city's streets are paved for the garage's cars, server side too.

The roof doesn't build a city of its own any more: while you're up there, the office lends it what you see out of the windows (the street, the city and the country past it) and takes it back when you come down.

- Own files:
  - `src/shared/city.ts`: the plan: the blocks (`BLOCKS`, the office's, the row across the street, city or park), the buildings on them (`LOTS`, drawn in the same order as the roof's city always was, so the skyline is the one it was), the parks' trees, the streets (`STREETS`, stretches crossing to crossing, only where they go somewhere: along a block of the town, through nothing, not into the country, and meeting the loop only where it's the office's street), the crossings and their paved corners, `onCityStreet` and `inTown`, and the runs the traffic drives (`RUNS`).
  - `src/client/world/town/`: `index.ts` (`buildTown`, `Town`, and the `town` fixture, which gives the office `town` and `lendOutlook`), `kit.ts` (paints, window textures, the wall and flat-strip builders, `rise`), `ground.ts` (sidewalks, streets, crossings, zebras, lots, lawns, the parks' trees, the street lamps), `buildings.ts` (the buildings, their roofs, masts with beacons, water towers, plant), `shops.ts` (the ground-floor shops' fronts and rooms, laid out by `shared/shops.ts`: see *Shops to walk into*), `furniture.ts` (curbs, street trees, benches, bins, bike stands, bus stops, hydrants, bollards), `pillars.ts` (advertising pillars pasted with posters of what's on in town, and newspaper boxes, in some of the bins' slots: `shared/streetside.ts` picks them by where they stand, so nothing else moves), `traffic.ts` (the city's cars).
  - `src/client/world/town/propcull.ts`: `PropCull`: small things far off aren't drawn. Every part of what's out there but the town (the landmarks' and halls' many small parts) is weighed by its size against its distance from the camera and, past about 40 of its own radii, put on a layer the camera doesn't see (its `visible` stays its owner's); `installTown` feeds it a quarter of the parts a frame. The forklift, pallets and trolleys of the DIY store are merged per material (`compact` in `world/baumarkt/models.ts`).
  - `src/client/features/town/index.ts`: `installTown`: each frame the cars and the lights (the `env` tick), the obstacles down here, lending the outlook to the roof and back, and culling the country from the roof.
  - `tests/city.test.ts`.
