#### Karaoke

The karaoke bar in the east of the centre (`ZONES.karaoke`), a part of its own (`features/karaoke/`, joined with `addBowlingPart`). **The bar** (`stage.ts`, `world.ts`): a black riser against the east wall with a running rainbow LED edge and a step up, a dark velvet backdrop in folds with ~400 twinkling sparkles, the big lyrics screen over it (6.2 m, 16:9) and a prompter tilted up at the singer on the stage's front edge (the same picture), two mic stands (mic 1 gold, mic 2 cyan for a duet), PA stacks and floor wedges, a box truss on two towers with four moving heads (additive beams that sweep figures of eight and change colour with the beat) and six par cans that chase, two coloured washes and a warm front light on the stage; an LED dance floor before it (36 instanced tiles stepping a checkerboard with the beat); a little bar against the mini golf room's wall (a padded front, a brass foot rail, an underglow, the back bar of bottles under a mirror, a tap, glasses, stools) under a pink-and-cyan KARAOKE neon with a mic and a note; six bistro tables with high stools, warm pendants and a SONGBUCH tent card each; the karaoke jockey's desk by the stage with a laptop and the song binder; the week's kings in a gold frame on the wall south of the stage. Cosmic bowling (`lights('cosmic')`): the pendants go out, UV trim glows on the tables and the bar, the beams and the sparkles go brighter, the bar's light goes violet.

**Songs** (`shared/karaoke-songs.ts`, `shared/karaoke-music.ts`): five short songs written for this office, words and tunes (no existing song's lyrics or melody anywhere): „Grüner Build“ (power ballad), „Merge-Konflikt-Blues“ (12-bar shuffle), „Feierabendbier auf dem Dach“ (Schlager, disco fox), „Strike!“ (stadium anthem), „Tango der leeren Kaffeemaschine“. Each is chords per bar, lyric lines split into syllables with `|`, and a melody per line (`G4:1 r:.5 _A4:1`); `timeline()` lays it out in beats (swung eighths for the blues), `songSeconds()` says how long. The band (`band.ts`, `instruments.ts`) plays it synthesized in the groove of its style (e-piano and brushes, walking boogie bass and organ, four-on-the-floor with brass, palm-muted and open power chords, the tango's 3-3-2 bandoneon and plucked strings) with a soft guide melody, a sixteenth at a time, from the PA's spot on your music volume, from the office's clock so every page is on the same beat. The screen (`screen.ts`, a canvas) lights the words up syllable by syllable, the next line under it, dots counting in, REFRAIN, a progress bar; or "Jetzt dran", the rating, the verdict; along the bottom "Als Nächstes: …" and the week's king. **YouTube karaoke videos**: anyone pastes a link (`parseKaraokeLink`, YouTube only, through `shared/embeds.ts`); it plays in YouTube's player laid over the big screen (over the prompter while you're on the stage), from the same moment for everyone, at a PA volume that never goes quiet in the centre (`client/tv.ts`'s `TvStreams` with its own `ScreenLook`); the singer's page says when it has ended (`karaoke.done`), at the latest after 15 minutes.

**Queue and stage** (`server/bowling/karaoke.ts`, `Karaoke`): **E** at a song book (the tables, the KJ desk) opens the song book (`ui.ts`, ✕ and Esc): the bar's songs, a link, the list (your own entries with ✕), the week's kings, and ending your song or giving your mic back. Two entries each, twenty in all. The first on the list is called up (`up`, a panel and a toast: "Du bist dran!") and has 60 s to take a mic (**E** at a stand), else they're skipped; taking it starts the song two seconds later for everyone. Anyone can take a free mic any time (open mic, or mic 2 for a duet); putting the lead's mic back (or `karaoke.stop`) ends the song; walking out of the bar with a mic puts it back; leaving the centre or the office lets go of your entries, your mic and your turn (`karaokeHooks`). **Voice**: whoever holds a mic and stands on the stage is on the PA: `Voice.setPa(ids)` (`client/voice-pa.ts`) plays them at full volume wherever you are in the centre, whatever the distance, with a little room reverb; without voice chat the singer's mouth moves with the words. The mic is in their hand (up at the mouth on the stage), and in yours in first person.

**Applause and rating**: **B** claps and **Shift+B** whoops (in the bar, or anywhere in the centre while a song's on), seen and heard by everyone; the 👏 emote is heard too; the bar applauds when a song ends. Then everyone but the singers rates it 1–5 🔥 (keys 1–5 or the panel's flames, 14 s; over at once when everyone's voted); the verdict and a fanfare. **Karaoke-König(in) der Woche** (`server/bowling/karaoke-board.ts`, `KaraokeCharts`): per person (`account:` or `name:`, like the soccer stats) the week's songs, the average and the points (every rated song's average added up), in `<dataDir>/karaoke.json` (0600); a new ISO week starts afresh and keeps last week's best. Guests and party guests may do all of it.

- Own files: `src/shared/karaoke.ts` (the layout in the zone, the state and messages, the timings, `parseKaraokeLink`, `weekKey`), `src/shared/karaoke-songs.ts`, `src/shared/karaoke-music.ts`, `src/server/bowling/karaoke.ts`, `src/server/bowling/karaoke-board.ts`, `src/server/ws/handlers/karaoke.ts` (`karaokeHandlers`, `karaokeHooks`), `src/client/features/karaoke/` (`index.ts`, `stage.ts`, `world.ts`, `screen.ts`, `band.ts`, `instruments.ts`, `sound.ts`, `people.ts`, `ui.ts` + `ui.css`), `src/client/voice-pa.ts`, `tests/karaoke.test.ts`.
- Hook lines: `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts` (`KaraokeClientMsg`, `KaraokeServerMsg`), `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts` (`...karaokeHandlers`), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`karaokeHooks` in `features`), `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`karaoke`, stopped in `stopFork`), `src/server/guests.ts` (the eight `karaoke.*` in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (`karaoke`, `karaoke.cheer`, `karaoke.rated` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/voice.ts` (`setPa`, the PA in `setVolume`, `src`/`wet` on a connection), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`karaoke`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installKaraoke`), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`.

#### Black-light mini golf

In the bowling centre's north-east corner (`ZONES.minigolf`, through `MINIGOLF_DOOR` past the lounge), a black-light mini golf room with nine holes, all different. Always black light, whatever the cosmic switch says: nothing in there is lit by the scene. Every surface is unlit, its shading baked into vertex colours (a soft violet light from above, `features/minigolf/look.ts` `bake`), what's painted with fluorescent paint glows in its own colour with an additive halo round it, and the static pieces of each hole are merged into one mesh per material (a hole draws in a handful of calls). Three violet point lights only for the people in there.

**The room** (`features/minigolf/room.ts`, built into the centre on the first entry through `addBowlingPart`): its own walls on the south and west (`MG_WALL`, the full height of the building, colliders) with the doorway (`MG_DOOR`, a glowing frame, a strip curtain in neon colours that sways, harder when someone walks through), outside a SCHWARZLICHT ★ MINIGOLF neon sign over the door and glowing stripes along the walls; inside murals in fluorescent paint all round, drawn once on canvases from a fixed seed (`paint.ts`: a jungle with vines, leaves, flowers and parrots on the west wall, outer space with planets, rings, nebulae and comets on the north, the deep sea with light rays, jellyfish, shoals, coral and kelp on the east, more space round the board, splatters everywhere), the black carpet with glowing confetti squiggles, a black ceiling at 3.4 m with rows of UV tubes and their glow, “Schläger & Bälle” (the stand just inside the door: a counter with putters in a rack, their heads glowing, and buckets of balls in every colour), the scorecard board on the south wall (`board.ts`, redrawn when it changes: who's out on the course, on which hole, how they stand; the best rounds ever and this week; the holes in one), and a corner to sit in north-west (benches with glowing edges, a big jellyfish lamp that floats and breathes).

**The nine holes** (`shared/minigolf-holes.ts`, each in its own frame, the tee near (0, 0) and up -z to the cup, placed in the room by `at`; `features/minigolf/course.ts` builds the felt at its heights, edged down to the floor, the rails with glowing tops, posts, the cup with a light round it, the tee mat and a sign with the number, name, par and a tip; `obstacles.ts` the rest): 1 Dschungel-Kurve (par 2), a dog-leg right with a bank in the corner, two bumpers and palms; 2 Windmühle (3), through the door under turning sails when they let you, then a green that breaks right; 3 Looping (2), a funnel into a loop the loop (too soft and it drops off the top or rolls back out), then gently uphill; 4 Schanze (3), up a ramp and through the air over a trench of glowing lava between glass sides (short of it: back to where you putted from); 5 Planet X (2), a striped planet with a ring and three tunnels: one comes out near the cup, one far from it, one back to the start; 6 Vulkan (3), putt up into the crater (too soft rolls back down, too hard flies over the rim), lava running down its sides; 7 Wackelbrücke (3), a bridge sliding to and fro over a glowing reef, the felt beyond lower; 8 Zwei Etagen (3), up a ramp to a deck with a hole in it, down a clear pipe (the ball seen going through) to a lower green with the cup; 9 Flipper (3), a pinball table: a spinner, pop bumpers that kick and flash, slingshots, the backbox. Walkways of ≥ 0.85 m between the holes, the door, the stand and the board kept clear (`tests/minigolf-course.test.ts`).

**The ball** (`shared/minigolf-physics.ts`, the course pieces in `shared/minigolf-course.ts`): pure and deterministic, stepped at 240 Hz. Rolling on felt (5/7 of gravity down a slope, rolling resistance plus a little drag), up and down planes and round profiles (the volcano, the funnel), off rails, posts and bumpers (their bounce, their kick), moving things against their own speed (the windmill's sail crossing the doorway, the spinner, the bridge and its rails, which carry the ball), off the felt into the air where it falls away faster than the ball can follow (a ramp's lip, the rim, an edge) and back down with a bounce, round the loop on its track (out of it when it's too slow over the top), into tunnels and holes and out elsewhere, over a cup's lip when too fast (a little off line, slower) or down it, and gone (a pit, off the course) back to where it was putted from. Everything that moves turns by the shared clock (`phase`: seconds into the hour of the office's clock, `clockSec`; every period divides the hour). A putt is where from, which way (the hole's frame), how hard and when: the office works it out (`simulate`) and every page rolls it the same, step by step, so everyone sees the same roll and the same windmill.

**Playing** (`features/minigolf/putting.ts`, `index.ts`): E at the stand takes a putter and a ball in a colour of your own (`MG_COLORS`, eight), E there again gives them back. E at the first tee starts a round with everyone there with a putter and no round going (a group, at most four, taking turns), or just for you; putting straight away plays alone and freely. E anywhere along your hole puts you over your ball (the putter's stance, the camera low behind the ball, a line on the felt the way you aim): the mouse or A D aims, holding Space runs the power meter up and down, letting go putts; the camera follows the ball (further back and higher while it's in a tunnel) and then you're over it again if it's still yours to play. Q picks the ball up (a “+”), E puts the putter down. A hole takes 7 strokes at most, then it's a “+” (counts 8). In a group whoever has putted least on the hole goes next, in the group's order; a turn not taken in 60 s passes on; the group goes on to the next hole once everyone's down. The bar at the top (the hole, its par, your stroke and total, whose turn, the meter) and the scorecard (Tab in the room, or E at the board: every card being played, the best rounds ever and this week, your own records; ✕, Tab or Esc close it, it never takes the mouse). Hole in one: a fanfare, confetti over the cup and a toast for everyone in the centre; a finished round: its total and whether it's your best, the card opens.

**The office's side** (`server/bowling/minigolf.ts`, `Minigolf` in the fork's context, `server/ws/handlers/minigolf.ts`): who has a putter and where their ball lies, the cards, groups and turns, the putts: only from someone in the centre, standing within `PUTT_REACH` of their ball, their turn, one at a time, the page's clock taken if it's within 1.5 s of the office's; the result goes to everyone in the centre at once (`mg.putt`), the card gets it once it has rolled out (a 100 ms tick). Leaving the centre or the office gives the putter back (`minigolfHooks`), and a group without them plays on. The records (`minigolf-records.ts`): per person (`account:<id>` or `name:<name>`) rounds, best round and when, best this week (ISO weeks), holes in one, in `<dataDir>/minigolf.json` (0600, written beside it and moved over it).

**Sound** (`features/minigolf/sound.ts`, `minigolf(kind)` and `setMinigolf(...)` on `OfficeSound`): the putter's tick, a rail's knock, a wooden bank, a bumper's boing, the pinball mushroom's pop, a sail's thwack, the cup's plop and a lip-out's rattle, landing, the hollow run through a tunnel, the loop's whoosh, the lava's sizzle and the reef's splash, the windmill's sails going by, the fanfares; the felt under the nearest rolling ball; a spacey pad with a glassy shimmer now and then, only in the room (and fading out a few metres past its door).

- Own files: `src/shared/minigolf.ts` (the room's numbers, the rules, the cards, `MgView`, the messages, `clockSec`, `weekOf`, `teeSpot`), `src/shared/minigolf-holes.ts`, `src/shared/minigolf-course.ts`, `src/shared/minigolf-physics.ts`, `src/server/bowling/minigolf.ts`, `src/server/bowling/minigolf-records.ts`, `src/server/ws/handlers/minigolf.ts`, `src/client/features/minigolf/` (`index.ts`, `room.ts`, `course.ts`, `obstacles.ts`, `balls.ts`, `putting.ts`, `look.ts`, `paint.ts`, `board.ts`, `show.ts`, `sound.ts`, `ui.ts`, `ui.css`), `tests/minigolf-course.test.ts`, `tests/minigolf-server.test.ts`.
- Hook lines: `src/shared/protocol.ts` (`MinigolfClientMsg`/`MinigolfServerMsg` in the unions), `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`minigolf`, started and stopped with the fork), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`minigolfHandlers`, `minigolfHooks`), `src/server/guests.ts` (`mg.*` in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (`mg`, `mg.putt` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installMinigolf`, `__minigolf`), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`minigolf`, `setMinigolf`), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a help row).
#### Bowling lanes

The six lanes in `ZONES.lanes` and the bowlers' area behind them (`ZONES.bowlers`), built into the centre's room through its seam (`addBowlingPart` from `features/bowlinggame/index.ts`), in real measures (`shared/bowling-game.ts`): 41.5-inch lanes of 39 maple and pine boards with the oil's sheen up to 40 feet, the seven target arrows in their V at 15 feet, the range dots at 7, the pin spots on the deck, a pale maple approach with its dots at 12 and 15 feet and the foul line with its little red eyes, shallow gutters either side, dark capping with a gold stripe between the lanes, red-faced kickbacks beside the decks, the black pit and its cushion. Over the pins the masking unit, a panel per lane with its number and a picture of its own (a rocket, a flamingo, pins in shades, a ringed planet, a lightning ball, a sunset), its light strip lighting the decks, the soffit above it, and behind the curtain the pinsetters. The pins are turned on a lathe from the real profile, two red neck stripes; the nine house balls (8–16 lbs) each have two colours and a finish (glitter that sparkles as it turns, marble, pearl, swirl), finger holes and their weight. Between each pair of lanes a ball return (the hood with its hand-dryer grille, a tray of every house ball, the lip the returning ball clunks onto), a scoring console with a screen per lane, a curved red bench (E sits you down, like the lounge's sofas: everyone sees who's on it), an overhead monitor per lane, and the league's big board on the west wall.

**Playing.** **E** at a lane's console joins its game (up to six; **X** leaves; **E** starts a new game once it's over, or alone; someone who keeps everyone waiting 90 s can be skipped there). **E** at the return picks a house ball (a window with ✕). When you're up, **E** on your approach: the camera drops in behind you, the mouse sets the line (a guide runs out to the arrows), **A D** slide you across, **W S** move your start up or back; hold **Space** (or the mouse button), the power meter runs, and moving the mouse (or **A D**) while you hold winds in the hook; let go: four steps and a slide, and the camera follows the ball to the pins. Start too far up with too much pace and the slide crosses the foul line: a foul (the buzzer, nothing counts). Back on your mark for your second ball once the pinsetter's done; **E** steps off.

**The ball and the pins** (`shared/bowling-sim.ts`, `BowlSim`): a small rigid-body simulation in the lane's frame at 300 steps a second, made of nothing but + − × ÷ and square roots (no trigonometry, random numbers or clock), so every machine gets the very same pins from the same throw. The ball skids, hooks and rolls: it carries a contact velocity (its spin), friction works on the slip (light in the oil, grabbing in the dry back end) until it rolls, so side spin turns into a hook late, less the faster it goes; over a lane edge it drops into the gutter. A pin standing is a disc of its belly, falling or lying a capsule from base to head swept round as it goes over, so falling pins take their neighbours (pin action); hit hard enough a pin goes over the way it was knocked, a nudge only wobbles it; kickbacks throw pins back, the pit swallows them. Tuned so a hook into the pocket strikes about two times in three, head-on leaves splits, and the leaves are the real ones (the 10 pin from a light hit, the 5 or 8 from a high one, 4-6 head-on). **Who decides:** the office (`server/bowling/lanes.ts`, `BowlingLanes`): the bowler's page sends only the throw (where they stood, how hard, the line, the hook, all clamped by `cleanThrow`), the office bowls it with the same simulation, counts the pins and sends the throw, the pins before and after and the lane's new sheet to everyone in the centre (`bowl.roll`, one small message a ball, no stream to throttle); every page plays it back (`features/bowlinggame/view.ts`), the bowler's page in step with their own steps, and settles on the office's pins at the end. Nobody can send pins, only a throw, and only when they're up and the last ball has been swept.

**What everyone sees** (`view.ts`, `pose.ts`): the bowler's push-away, swing, steps and slide on their character (`Person.setWorkout`), the ball in the hand of whoever's up, the ball rolling and turning down the lane with its rumble (pitch and loudness by speed, a rattle in the gutter), the pins' clatter (a big crash for a strike), the pinsetter (the table lifts what's standing, the sweep bar comes down in front of the deadwood and rakes it into the pit, the table sets the standing pins back or brings a fresh rack down), and the ball popping up the return a moment later. Then the monitor's celebration: STRIKE!, DOUBLE!, TURKEY! (and confetti), HAMBONE!, PERFEKT! 300, SPARE!, SPLIT…, GUTTER…, FOUL!, with a fanfare (or a sad trombone). Everyone in the centre sees every lane; several lanes can bowl at once.

**Scoring** (`shared/bowling-score.ts`): ten-pin rules: strikes and spares with their bonus balls, the tenth frame's extra balls (the rack set fresh after each mark), fouls (no pins; after a foul on a first ball the rack is set again, and all ten on the second is a spare), splits (the head pin down and the pins left apart, or two side by side in the middle row with the pin ahead down; the 2-8, 3-9, 8-9 and 9-10 aren't) circled on the sheet. Turns as in a bowling centre: a frame each in order, whoever joins late bowls their missed frames first; leaving the centre or the office takes you off your lane (`bowlingGameHooks`). The monitors draw the classic sheet (`monitor.ts`), in neon under the black light.

**Cosmic bowling** (the centre's switch, `lights('cosmic')`): the lanes go dark with neon arrows, dots, edges and stars, the approaches' dots and the foul line glow, a violet strip down each gutter, the pins glow violet-white with hot pink stripes, the balls in their own colours, the masking unit's pictures fluoresce, and every screen turns neon.

**The league** (`shared/bowling-league.ts`, `server/bowling/league.ts`, saved as `<dataDir>/bowling.json`, 0600, at most 20 000 games): every finished game counts, by who bowled it (`account:<id>`, or `name:<name>` on the shared password). The week runs Monday to Sunday on Berlin's clock; its table ranks people by the average of their best three games of the week (a game short counts 0), and last week's winner wears a gold crown over their head in the centre (`crowned`) and is on the board. All time: the high games, the best averages (from five games), the most strikes, the perfect games. The big board on the west wall shows the week and the records; **E** there opens the window (Woche / Allzeit / Meine Spiele, ✕ top right).

- Own files: `src/shared/bowling-game.ts` (the lane's measures and marks, the pins' spots, `BALLS`, where the returns, consoles, benches, monitors and the board are, `ThrowParams`, `cleanThrow`, `slideEnd`, the views and the messages `BowlingGameClientMsg`/`BowlingGameServerMsg`, all `bowl.*`), `src/shared/bowling-score.ts`, `src/shared/bowling-sim.ts`, `src/shared/bowling-league.ts`; `src/server/bowling/lanes.ts`, `src/server/bowling/league.ts`, `src/server/ws/handlers/bowlinggame.ts` (the handlers, `bowlingGameHooks`, one `BowlingLanes` and `BowlingLeague` per office); `src/client/features/bowlinggame/` (`index.ts` install, the seam, the kinds `bowlconsole`, `bowlreturn`, `bowlapproach`, `bowlboard`, `bowlbench` and `Interactable.bowlLane`, the crowns; `lanes3d.ts`, `machine.ts`, `furniture.ts`, `props.ts` (pin and balls), `crown.ts`, `benches.ts`, `view.ts`, `pose.ts`, `play.ts` (`Bowler`, the activity `bowling`), `monitor.ts`, `ui.ts` + `ui.css`, `sound.ts`); `src/client/lab/bowling.html` + `bowling.ts` (a lab page: `view=bowler|deck|deckside|seats|board|top|side|monitor|returns`, `throw=strike|pocket|gutter|split|none`, `t=`, `cosmic=1`, for headless screenshots); `tests/bowling-score.test.ts` (300, all spares, gutter game, the tenth frame, fouls, splits), `tests/bowling-sim.test.ts` (determinism, skid-hook-roll, gutters, strikes and splits, second balls, fouls), `tests/bowling-league.test.ts` (Berlin's week, the tables, the champion, the file), `tests/bowling-lanes.test.ts` (turns, the sweep's wait, perfect and gutter games, fouls, late joiners, leaving, six a lane, skipping, guests).
- Hooks in shared lists: `src/shared/protocol.ts` (`BowlingGameClientMsg`/`BowlingGameServerMsg` in the unions), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`...bowlingGameHandlers`, `bowlingGameHooks` in `features`), `src/server/guests.ts` (the eight `bowl.*` in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (the six `bowl.*` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installBowlingGame`, `__bowlinggame`), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`bowlingLane(kind, at, strength)`, `bowlRoll(id, at, speed, gutter)`), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row).

### The Schallwerk

Across the street east of the gym (`VENUE_BOX` x 89..137, z 36..68, its front on the street; the farm moved east for it), a concert hall one night and a club the next: the SCHALLWERK, with fully kitted-out rehearsal rooms and a studio. The plan all four parts build on is `src/shared/venue.ts` (`VENUE = '@venue'`, the box, the room, `ZONES`: who builds where, `WING_DOOR`, the rehearsal rooms, `INSTRUMENT_SPOTS`, `STAGE_HEIGHT`, `VenueMode`), the seam they join by is `src/client/world/venue/parts.ts` (`addVenuePart({ build, use, hint, onMessage, update, place, mode })`, `venueSynth`, `venueLevel`). This section is the building's part (the house outside and in, the place, the foyer, the bar, FOH and the light show, backstage); the instruments, the rehearsal wing and the show have their own subsections below.

**The building** (`world/venue/exterior.ts`, `exterior-yard.ts`, `signs.ts`). An old power station of sooty red brick: dark pilasters, a stone plinth and cornice, tall arched factory windows in their glazing bars (dark old glass by day, warm light and the crowd's heads at night), a sawtooth roof whose glazed north faces glow at night, the old chimney at the back with SCHALLWERK painted down it, a red aircraft light and an uplight; SCHALLWERK in big red neon on a steel lattice on the roof's front edge (its glow on the night). The entrance in a brick portal with a round arch and a lit fanlight, steel-and-glass doors that slide apart for anyone near (`site.doors`), the foyer seen through them, EINGANG over them; over it the marquee: a steel canopy with chaser bulbs that run at night, downlights, SCHALLWERK on its sides and on its front the letter board with what's on tonight (`world/venue/bill.ts`: HEUTE: KONZERT / HEUTE: CLUB by the house's mode, or the show part's bill), tie rods to the wall. Lit poster cases either side (made-up bands), gooseneck lanterns washing the brick. West of the doors the smokers' beer garden (`BEER_GARDEN`): gravel, a low steel fence, three sets of beer benches, a fire bowl whose flames flicker and light the wall at night, string lights from poles to the wall, barrel planters, an ashtray, a BIERGARTEN · RAUCHER sign. East of them the band's nightliner (`TOUR_BUS`: three axles, tinted windows, the upper deck's curtains glowing at night, SCHALLWERK ON TOUR on its display). Round the east side at the back the loading dock (`DOCK`): a platform at truck height with its hazard edge and rubber bumpers, steps, the roll-up door, flight cases, the dock lamp, ANLIEFERUNG · BÜHNE. Built into the street's outlook (`venueOut` in `world/venue/index.ts`), so it's there from every floor's street, the windows and the roof; one coarse collider for the house (and the chimney's), the yard's own. The street furniture keeps off it (`built` in `shared/streetside.ts`), the golf balls and the loop's trees keep off it and its yard (`neighbourBoxes`), the farm's signpost stands past it, DER BRECHER's keep-outs have it. The minimap: 🎸 Schallwerk, the house, the marquee, the garden, the bus and the dock.

**The place** (`client/venue/place.ts`, `VenuePlace`, like the bowling centre's). **E** at the doors goes in (`floor.go` to `VENUE`), **E** at the doors inside back out onto the street of the floor you came from (`sessionStorage`), in front of the doors; a reload brings you back (`backInPlace`); the last floor off the building sends people in there to the lobby. Whereabouts by zone in German (`venueWhereabouts`: im Foyer, an der Bar, am Mischpult, vor der Bühne, auf der Bühne (on the riser), am DJ-Pult, Backstage, im Proberaum N, im Studio, bei den Proberäumen), the people list says 🎸 Schallwerk, the minimap shows them at the house. Guests and party guests may do all of it. On arriving in the office every page asks for the house (`venue.hello`), so the letter board and everyone's shirts and stamps are right from the start.

**Inside** (`world/venue/room.ts` and the rest of `world/venue/`, built the first time anyone goes in): polished dark concrete; raw brick, painted black to 3.5 m in the hall with a steel angle where the paint stops, the old arched windows high up blacked out; acoustic panels over the bar and along the wing's wall; riveted Pratt roof trusses under a dark corrugated ceiling; enamel pendants as the house lights (and their pools on the floor). The gallery over the foyer (`GALLERY`, y 4.4) with its balcony rail toward the stage, · S C H A L L W E R K · on its fascia, timber boards and warm downlights underneath, on four pillars (`PILLARS_X`). The house's brick on the hall's side of the rehearsal wing's east wall (the wing's own, with `WING_DOOR` into the foyer) and the artists' door (`BACKSTAGE_DOOR`) from the wing's corridor into backstage, BACKSTAGE · Nur Crew & Bands over it; the hall's back wall behind the stage (`BACK_WALL`, z 11..11.25, full height) with the band's opening over the stairs' landing and drapes; the doors back out with AUSGANG; green exit signs; the house's mark painted over the doors. Parts are built into the house with it (`venueParts(onJoin)`; a part joining later is built at once), get every message, every frame inside, `place(inside)` and `mode(...)` (on coming in and on every switch); E and the hint go to them for anything that isn't the house's; their own kinds, or the house's generic `venuepart`. Everything you use has an invisible pick box for the crosshair (`world/venue/pick.ts`), and the light and the air (beams, haze, confetti, sparks) let the crosshair through (`noPick`).

**The foyer** (`foyer.ts`, laid out in `shared/venue-house.ts`): the box office (`KASSE`, a glazed booth, Mia behind it, the stamp pad, the ABENDKASSE board: Eintritt frei): **E** gets a stamp on your hand, the house's mark in cyan that glows, for everyone to see, all night (`STAMP_MS`); the cloakroom (`GARDEROBE`, Jo, rails of hangers with strangers' coats): **E** hands your jacket in for a numbered ticket (it hangs on the rail in your colour for everyone) or gets it back; the merch stand (`MERCH_TABLE`, `MERCH_WALL`, Sam in the shirt): **E** opens it, the Logo-Shirt, the Tour-Shirt (the dates on the back) and the Hoodie (hood, drawstrings, pocket) to put on or take off (`client/venue/wear.ts`: over the torso and the arms, the logo on the chest, on everyone you see, in there or anywhere: a shirt and a stamp go along with you), and the tour poster as a PNG (`poster.ts`); the photo booth (`FOTOBOX`, red velvet, FOTOBOX in bulbs): **E** takes four shots of you and up to three people standing by it (`client/venue/booth.ts`, their looks and their shirts and stamps, each shot its own look), the strip to download. High tables, gig posters on the walls and pillars, its own warm light. What people have on is kept per person (account or name) in `<dataDir>/venue.json` (0600), so reloads and restarts keep it.

**The bar** (`bar.ts`, `ZONES.bar`): a riveted black counter with a timber top, an amber underglow, a brass foot rail, three taps, glasses, a till, the back bar with lit coolers, bottles backlit under a mirror, BAR in red neon, the chalk board, cage pendants, Ronja. **E** anywhere along it orders (`BAR_MENU`: Zwickl vom Fass, Helles, Radler, Mate, Spezi, Cola, Gin Tonic, Spritz, Obstler, Energy, Sprudel; all held like the fridge's things, so along to every floor; the booze's cut-off holds).

**Front of house** (`foh.ts`, `ZONES.foh`): a platform with a rail round three sides, the sound desk (faders, live meters) and the light desk (its screen showing the mode, the scene, the beat), outboard LEDs. **E** at the light desk opens the **Lichtpult** (`client/venue/ui.ts`, live): **Konzert ⇄ Club** for the whole house (`venue.mode`, at most once in `MODE_COOLDOWN_MS`; to everyone in the office, for the letter board; the lights go to the mode's own `MODE_LIGHTS`), a scene (`SCENES`: auto, warm, rot, blau, UV, strobo, blackout; `venue.lights`), the lasers, the mirror ball, and the effects (`venue.fx`, each reloading on its own, `FX_COOLDOWN_MS`): CO₂ jets from the front truss, the confetti cannons, the cold sparkler fountains on the stage's front lip, the haze machine. **E** at the sound desk: announcements over the PA (`venue.announce`, `ANNOUNCEMENTS`, a chime for everyone inside).

**The light show** (`lighting.ts`, `rig.ts`, `led.ts`, `fx.ts`): the light desk's state turned into a look each frame; `auto` follows `venueLevel()` (parts.ts): a beat is the level jumping over its running average; on it the colours step on, the blinders hit, the LED wall rings out; the louder the faster the heads sweep. A concert: house lights low, the stage washed warm white with colour drifting through, the front light; a club: dark, deep colours, the mirror ball's spots sweeping the hall, laser fans over everyone's heads (a sheet now and then), more haze. Everything runs on the office's clock, so everyone inside sees the same; effects play from when the office says they went off (late arrivals see the rest). The rig (all above 6 m, the building's): five box trusses on chain hoists (three over the stage, two over the hall), 14 moving heads, PAR washes, four blinders, UV bars, three real spot washes on the stage, the front light, a floor colour, the strobe, the mirror ball with its pin spot, the laser unit, the hazers, and the LED wall on the back wall over the backline (x -7.5..12.5, y 4.9..8.5: tonight's act from the bill in a concert, a spectrum and the house's mark in a club). The hall's air (hemisphere and ambient) is the look's (`applyMood`).

**Backstage** (`backstage.ts`, `ZONES.backstage`, under its own low ceiling): the green room (leather sofas: **E** sits you down, like the bowling lounge's; a low table with bottles and a fruit bowl, a rug, the make-up mirrors framed in bulbs, a rail of stage clothes, the setlist taped to the wall, band stickers), the rider (`RIDER`, a glass-door fridge plastered with stickers, its list on the door: **E** takes a drink, `RIDER_MENU`), flight cases, a cable spool, the loading door, bare bulbs. **The band's way up**: steel stairs (`STAGE_STAIRS`, `stairSteps()`, 0.2 m risers) climbing north to the landing in the back wall's opening at the riser's height (`STAIRS_LANDING`, z 11, `STAGE_HEIGHT`), ON STAGE over it.

**Sound** (`client/venue/sound.ts`, `ctx.sound.venue`): the doors (push bar, the hall's noise through them), the stamp, a coat on a hanger, a merch bag and the card reader, the shutter, the tap, the rider fridge, the till, the light desk's buttons, the switch to a club (a bass drop and a riser) or a concert (a cheer swelling), CO₂, confetti (and the paper fluttering), the sparklers' crackle, the haze machine, the PA's chime; inside, the air: a crowd talking (louder in the foyer), glasses at the bar, someone laughing, the hall's room tone. It fades away by itself outside.

**Seams with the other parts.** The gig calendar (`client/venue/gigbill.ts`, on `onGigsChanged` from `features/venueshow/gigs.ts`): the gig on now as HEUTE: … on the letter board and the LED wall, the next one as DEMNÄCHST: …, the calendar's posters (`gigPoster`) in the façade's four cases (`setVenueBill` in world/venue/bill.ts; without gigs the mode's words and the house's own posters). A gig starting switches the house to its kind (`showOf(ctx).onGigStart` → `gigStarted` in `server/ws/handlers/venue.ts` → `VenueHouse.gigStarts`, past the light desk's cooldown, to everyone). In a rehearsal room the house's crowd, bar and effects are kept out (`muffleFor` from `shared/proberaum.ts`; the instruments and the show muffle their own). The wing's east wall is the wing's (`features/proberaum/shell.ts`, x -11.2..-11) with the artists' door cut into it for backstage; the building only faces it with its brick on the hall's side and frames the artists' door; the place sets `player.room` only coming in and on a map change, so the wing's own camera room isn't fought over. `venueLevel()`: the light show follows what the instruments and the DJ booth report. `mode(...)`: every part is told concert or club. What the building puts where others build, so they keep it clear: four sparkler fountains (26 × 20 cm, no collider) on the riser's front lip at x -8.5, -3.2, 8.2, 13.4 (z 4.12); the stairs' landing meets the riser's back edge at x 11.4..13.4 (the riser should be solid up to z 11 there, no backline in front of it); the LED wall over the backline above 4.7 m; the back wall at z 11..11.25 behind the stage and the DJ booth; the artists' door `BACKSTAGE_DOOR` in the wing's east wall (the corridor stays open there); the wing's outer brick (its rooms are the rehearsal wing's).

- Own files: `src/shared/venue-house.ts` (the house's layout, `houseSolids`, `walkways`, the menus, the merch, `VenueWear`, the scenes and effects and their timings, the messages, `venueWhereabouts`, the yard round the building), `src/server/venue/place.ts` (`VENUE_ARRIVAL`, `venueView`, `backInVenue`, `VenueHouse`: the mode, the light desk, the effects, the announcements, what people have on, `venue.json`), `src/server/ws/handlers/venue.ts` (`venueHandlers`), `src/client/world/venue/` (`index.ts` the `venueOut` fixture, `exterior.ts`, `exterior-yard.ts`, `signs.ts`, `bill.ts`, `room.ts`, `foyer.ts`, `bar.ts`, `foh.ts`, `backstage.ts`, `rig.ts`, `led.ts`, `fx.ts`, `lighting.ts`, `pick.ts`, `interior.ts`; `parts.ts` is the plan's), `src/client/venue/` (`place.ts`, `hints.ts`, `staff.ts`, `seats.ts`, `wear.ts`, `booth.ts`, `poster.ts`, `ui.ts` + `ui.css`, `sound.ts`), `src/client/lab/venue.html` + `venue.ts` + `venue-in.ts` (the building outside and in by eye: `what=out|in`, `view`, `night`, `mode`, `scene`, `fx`), `tests/venue-building.test.ts`.
- Hooks in upstream and other fork files:
  - `src/shared/protocol/fork.ts`: `VenueHouseClientMsg`, `VenueHouseServerMsg`. `src/server/ws/handlers/fork.ts`: `...venueHandlers`. `src/server/fork/office.ts`: `venue` (`VenueHouse`), `placeView`, `ENTRY` (`VENUE_ARRIVAL`), `enteredPlace` (sends `venue.house`), `backInPlace`, `stopFork`. `src/server/office/floors.ts`: the last floor off sends people in the Schallwerk to the lobby. `src/server/guests.ts`: the eight `venue.*` in `GUEST`; `src/server/party.ts`: `venue.mode`, `venue.lights`, `venue.fx`, `venue.announce`, `venue.wear`, `venue.house` in `PARTY_SEES`.
  - `src/shared/streetside.ts`: `built` keeps the furniture off the house. `src/shared/coaster-keepout.ts`: the Schallwerk's box. `src/client/world/outside.ts`: `neighbourBoxes` has the house (to the chimney's top) and its `YARD`. `src/client/world/scenic/road.ts`: the farm's signpost 34 m into its stretch, past the Schallwerk's front.
  - `src/client/world/office/build.ts`: `venueOut` in `downstairs(...)`. `src/client/features/places/index.ts`: `venue` (a `VenuePlace`), the kinds `venue`, `venuekasse`, `venuecoat`, `venuemerch`, `venuebooth`, `venuebar`, `venuerider`, `venuelight`, `venuemix`, `venueseat`, `venuepart`. `src/client/main.ts`: `__office.venue`. `src/client/features/bungee/index.ts`: no bungee from in there. `src/client/features/coaster/index.ts`: DER BRECHER isn't drawn inside the Schallwerk (nor the bowling centre, whose interior it ran through).
  - `src/client/features/minimap/pois.ts` (🎸 Schallwerk, `placeSpot`), `atlas.ts`, `tests/minimap.test.ts`.
  - `src/server/fork/office.ts` `startFork`: `showOf(ctx).onGigStart` (a gig starting switches the house). `src/client/features/proberaum/shell.ts`: the artists' door (`BACKSTAGE_DOOR`) in the wing's east wall. `src/client/venue/gigbill.ts` reads the show's `features/venueshow/gigs.ts`.
  - `src/client/sound/index.ts`: `venue`. `src/client/ui/whereabouts.ts`, `src/client/ui/people.ts`, `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`: words.
#### The show

The Schallwerk's crowd, its DJ booth and its gig calendar (`ZONES.floor` and `ZONES.djbooth` in `shared/venue.ts`), a part of its own (`features/venueshow/`, joined with `addVenuePart`). Guests and party guests may do all of it but edit the calendar, which is the team's.

**The crowd** (`shared/venueshow-crowd.ts`, `features/venueshow/crowd.ts`): up to 80 people on the floor, drawn like the city's passers-by (one instanced mesh per body part for all of them, faces and hands only within 26 m, nothing while you're in a rehearsal room). Nothing of them is on the wire: each page works them out the same from the house's mode, the gigs, the hour, the beat and what people do. How many come (`crowdTarget`): a few by day, more in the evening, a full house while a gig from the calendar is on, 50 on a club night with music, 30 to 72 with the stage loud (the stage's level is `venueLevel()` minus the booth's own). Where they stand (`crowdSpots`, worked out once a mode): a jittered grid inside `crowdArea(mode)`, never on the `WALKWAYS` (the back from the foyer to the bar and the wing, the bar's front, the poster wall) and never closer than 0.66 m; in concert mode a crowd pressing up to the barrier in front of the stage's middle, packed at the front, thinning to the sides and the back; in club mode all over the floor. Coming in, the crowd's already there; after that new people walk in from the back and leave that way. **Concert** (`concertMood`): facing the band; idle (shifting about, chatting) with the stage quiet, swaying with lighters and phone lights up in the slow bits, nodding with fists up, the front rows jumping and both fists up when it's loud, on a beat counted from the stage level's onsets; when a song of more than 12 s ends the house applauds and cheers, and after a long one now and then chants "Zugabe!" by itself. The Wellenbrecher (`BARRIER`: steel crowd barriers on their footplates, a collider) stands in front of the stage with security (`SECURITY`, a `Person` with arms crossed, SECURITY on the back) in the pit. **Club**: the barrier sinks into the floor segment by segment and security goes; everyone dances to the beat (the DJ's set or the house mix), each their own moves by the part of the set and the phrase (`moveFor`: bounce, fist pump, both hands up waving, two-step, headbang, pointing up, jumping, clapping overhead), jumping as a drop lands while confetti rains from the ceiling, three beach balls bouncing over their hands. **Real people**: the crowd steps aside for every player on the floor (`stepAside`), puts their hands up under a crowd-surfer, turns to a pit.

**What you do in it** (keys in a strip along the bottom while you're on the floor, `crowdKeys`): **Space** in the crowd is a pogo as well as a jump (`show.act` pogo); two or more people pogoing within 3.6 m of each other in 2.6 s open a pit (`findPit`): the crowd inside makes a ring round it, a third of them run a circle pit, the rest of the ring jumps and the bodies thump. **B** claps, **Shift+B** starts "Zugabe!" (the crowd joins in), **L** held up a lighter (a phone light in club mode; your arm up with a flame, for everyone), **K** a concert photo: a flash everyone sees (a point light where you stand, a shutter and the flash whining), the frame copied off the canvas into a print with its caption (`souvenirs.ts`), popping up for a moment, kept in your souvenirs to save as a JPEG. **E** at the stage's front edge (`DIVE_EDGE`; an invisible strip there, only usable while you stand up on the stage) is a stage dive (`surf.ts`, the player rig): with 22 or more in the crowd its hands catch you and pass you back over their heads (lying on your back for everyone, `show.surf`) to the back of the floor and set you down; with nobody there you belly-flop in front of the stage. A Wall of Death (`show.wod`, from the stage or the DJ's desk, once in 40 s): the crowd parts down the middle, waits, and runs at each other into one big pit. Beach balls (`shared/venueshow-balls.ts`): **E** (or jumping into one) bats it; only the hit goes over the wire, and every page and the office fly it the same from there, bounce by bounce over the crowd's hands (each bounce's kick from the hit's count), down onto a walkway and still; a ball nobody hit is thrown in afresh every ten minutes on the office's clock. The office checks a hit against its own reckoning of where the ball is (`hitBall`: within 2.6 m, the speed clamped). Everything is relayed to everyone in the house from where the office has you (not where the page says), rate-limited per person (`ACT_EVERY`). When a gig is on, 20 s in the house puts its ticket stub (Nº, Stehplatz) in your souvenirs (`localStorage`).

**The DJ booth** (`booth.ts`; `BOOTH_RISER`, `BOOTH_STAIRS`, `BOOTH_DESK`, `DJ_SPOT`, `BOOTH_SCREEN`): its own riser (1 m) stage right with five lit steps up its west side and a handrail, the desk with two CDJs (lit jog wheels turning, screens), a four-channel mixer with VU meters, a laptop on a stand showing two decks' waveforms, headphones, a gooseneck lamp, an LED strip under the desk's edge, SCHALLWERK lit on its front, SCHALLWERK running in LEDs across the riser's front with a chase underneath, two monitor wedges, two moving heads on poles throwing beams over the floor, and an LED screen behind the DJ (spectrum, rising bars and GET READY through a build, rings bursting at the drop, waves in a breakdown, DJ <name> and the set's title). **E** at the decks takes them (`venuedj.take`, one DJ at a time, only standing at them; you're put behind them facing the floor) and opens the DJ desk (`ui.ts`, ✕/Esc): a YouTube, SoundCloud or Mixcloud set for the whole house (`venuedj.play`, the roof's `parseDjSetUrl`, saved in `<dataDir>/venue-dj.json`), heard by the office for its beats like the roof's (`VenueDjBooth` with its own `DjBeatsJobs`, `GET /api/venue/beats`) or tapped in; back to the house mix; the house mix's style (Deep House 122, Techno 130, Nu Disco 116, `venuedj.house`); 📯 (or **H**), 🚀 Drop! (the house mix builds four bars from the next bar, then drops, for everyone: `house.dropAt`), 💀 Wall of Death; skipping through the set (`venuedj.seek`, the DJ's: ⏪ 30 s, ⏩ 30 s/2/5 min and a slider, the roof's `skipControls`); give the decks back (**Q**, or walk off the booth; the set plays on; the last one out of the house takes it off). The DJ moves to the beat on whoever's at the decks (`djPose`, as the roof's DJ, through `Person.setWorkout`). **The house mix** (`shared/venueshow-mix.ts`, `music.ts`): generated with Web Audio on the office's clock (a 64-bar cycle: intro, build, drop, breakdown; four on the floor, claps, hats, an offbeat, rolling or octave bass, minor-seventh stabs, pads, a riser and a snare roll into the drop, a crash on it), plays by itself on club nights and whenever someone's at the decks without a set. The set plays in the site's own player (`client/djset.ts`'s `DjSetPlayer`) only while you're in the house; both go at the house's own volume (`VenueDjState.volume`/`volumeBy`, 0–200 %, a slider with 🔇 and 🪩 Disco! on the DJ desk for the team, `venuedj.volume` in `TEAM_ONLY`, kept in `<dataDir>/venue-volume.json`; guests see it), muffled (a low-pass, much quieter) in a rehearsal room (`venueRoomAt`). The booth reports `setVenueLevel('dj', …)` from its beat, for the building's lights.

**The gig calendar** (`server/venue/gigs.ts`, `GigCalendar`; `<dataDir>/venue-gigs.json`, 0600, written beside it and moved over it): a gig is a title, Konzert or Club, start and end (real time, to the minute; three hours unless it says, twelve at most; not in the past, not over a year ahead, never two at once), a line for the poster, its look (`POSTER_STYLES`: Plakat, Neon, Punk, Rave, Retro, Minimal) and colour, and who put it in; gone a month after it's over. Anyone may read it from anywhere (`gig.list`, and the `gigs` message to the whole office whenever it changes: the posters hang outside too); the team puts gigs in, changes and cancels them (`gig.save`, `gig.delete`: `TEAM_ONLY`). Every 5 s the office looks whether one has started: once each, even across a restart (`announced`), everyone in the office gets `gig.started`: a banner on every floor ("Jetzt im Schallwerk: …", its poster, **Hingehen** or **Enter** does `floor.go` to the house; "Es geht los!" in there, with the crowd's roar and confetti). The poster wall (`POSTER_WALL`, on the wing's wall facing into the hall, under PROGRAMM, lit by two spots) hangs the next eight gigs' posters (`posters.ts`: drawn on a canvas, bold type, the date and time, the line, in the gig's look; empty slots say DEMNÄCHST); **E** there opens the programme (`ui.ts`, ✕/Esc): what's coming with its poster, the gig on now, the team's form with a live poster preview, and the souvenirs tab (ticket stubs and photos, each with 💾).

**Sound** (`sound.ts`, `ctx.sound.venueShow`): the house mix on the music bus (party volume, a limiter, the walls' low-pass); the crowd on the effects volume: a murmur by how full it is, a brighter cheering layer swelling with the show, applause and the roar (the soccer hall's crowd recipes), clapping and whoops, the "Zu-ga-be!" chant (a dozen synthesized voices on three syllables, clapping along), the pit's thumps, a shutter and flash, a beach ball's pock, the air horn, a thud for a dive nobody caught.

- Own files: `src/shared/venueshow.ts` (the layout in its zones, the DJ booth's state, `Gig` and `cleanGig`, the crowd's acts and their rate limits, the messages), `src/shared/venueshow-crowd.ts`, `src/shared/venueshow-balls.ts`, `src/shared/venueshow-mix.ts`; `src/server/venue/show.ts` (`VenueShow`, `VenueDjBooth`), `src/server/venue/gigs.ts`, `src/server/ws/handlers/venueshow.ts` (`venueShowHandlers`, `venueShowHooks`, `showOf`, `startVenueShow`/`stopVenueShow`), `src/server/http/routes/venueshow.ts`; `src/client/features/venueshow/` (`index.ts` the install and the part, `house.ts` every frame in the house, `crowd.ts`, `booth.ts`, `floor.ts`, `dj.ts`, `music.ts`, `sound.ts`, `surf.ts`, `posters.ts`, `gigs.ts`, `souvenirs.ts`, `ui.ts` + `ui.css`); `tests/venueshow-crowd.test.ts`, `tests/venueshow-server.test.ts`.
- Hook lines in shared lists: `src/shared/protocol.ts` (`VenueShowClientMsg`/`VenueShowServerMsg` in the unions), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`...venueShowHandlers`, `venueShowHooks` in `features`), `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`startVenueShow`/`stopVenueShow` in `startFork`/`stopFork`), `src/server/http/routes/index.ts` (`venueShowRoutes.beats`), `src/server/guests.ts` (`show.*`, `gig.list`, `venuedj.*` in `GUEST`; `gig.save`, `gig.delete`, `venuedj.volume` in `TEAM_ONLY`; `/api/venue/beats` in `guestMayFetch`), `src/server/party.ts` (`show`, `show.act`, `show.surf`, `show.ball`, `show.wod`, `venuedj`, `venuedj.horn`, `gigs`, `gig.started` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installVenueShow`, `__venueshow`), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`venueShow`), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`.
- **Seams for the building** (the integrator wires them):
  - The house's mode on a gig: `showOf(ctx).onGigStart = (gig) => …` (server, `ws/handlers/venueshow.ts`) is called once when a gig starts; the building switches `venue.mode` to `gig.kind` there (`'konzert'` or `'club'`, the same `VenueMode`). The show's own `gig.started` goes to the whole office regardless.
  - The façade's poster frames and marquee: `upcomingGigs()`, `liveGig()`, `onGigsChanged(fn)` and `gigPoster(gig, width)` from `client/features/venueshow/gigs.ts` (a canvas to put on a `CanvasTexture`; the calendar is on every page, in or out of the house).
  - The light show: `venueLevel()` has the booth's `'dj'` share; the beat itself is `__venueshow.house.frame` (a `MixFrame`, the roof DJ's `DjFrame` shape) if the building wants to flash on the kicks.
  - Interaction kinds `venuedj`, `venueposter`, `venuedive`, `venueball` are the show's own (`ctx.interactions.define`), their meshes in `room.group` carry `userData.interact` (first person); the part's `use`/`hint` answer for them too, for the building's generic kind.
  - The instruments part may call a Wall of Death from its band overlay by sending `{ t: 'show.wod' }` (the office checks the caller stands on the stage).
#### The instruments

Part of the Schallwerk (`shared/venue.ts`, the plan: `ZONES.stage`, `INSTRUMENT_SPOTS`, `InstrumentNote`, `DRUM_PIECES`), joined into the house with `addVenuePart` from `features/instruments/index.ts`.

**The stage** (`ZONES.stage`, `features/instruments/stage.ts`): a 1.2 m riser across the back of the hall facing the floor (-z): black 2 × 1 m stage platforms with their seams, scuffs and spike tape in each player's colour, a rounded lip with white glow tape, a pleated black skirt, five steps up at stage left (`STAGE_STEPS`, x -11..-9.8, taped noses, a handrail on the wall side), all of it colliders you walk up and stand on. On it the backline: the drum riser (`DRUM_RISER`, 0.28 m, a step up, a rug), two full stacks behind the guitarists, the bass rig, the stage piano's upper tier and the organ's rotating speaker, wedges at the three mics, the bass and the keys and one beside the drummer, side-fills on both wings, pedalboards, the stage box with the cables taped down to it, setlists, towels and water. The back edge (z 10.2..11) is left clear for the building's stairs up from backstage.

**The instruments** (`stations.ts`, `gear/`): one at every spot, in the spot's frame (the player at the origin facing +z): a five-piece kit with throne, hi-hat, crash and ride (`gear/kit.ts`, a finish per room: black on the stage, red, blue, pearl, wood; the bass drum's front head printed), a guitar or bass on its stand (`gear/strings.ts`: extruded double-cutaway bodies, sunburst and goldtop on canvases, real fret spacing, pickups, strings) with a combo behind you in the rehearsal rooms (`gear/amps.ts`, also the stacks, the bass rig, wedges, side-fills, the rotary cab), a 61-key board on an X stand whose keys go down as they're played (`gear/keys.ts`, instanced keys), a mic on a boom stand. Static parts merged by colour, each model built once and cloned; colliders clear of the spot.

**Playing** (`play.ts`, `overlay.ts`, the key maps in `shared/instruments-play.ts`): **E** at an instrument asks the office for it (`instr.take`); once it says it's yours (`instr.state`) you're held at the spot (`player.rig`, facing the instrument, the drummer sitting) and your keys play it (activity `instrument`, before the office's own keys); **E** or **Esc** (on the keyboard **Esc** or **Backspace**, its **E** is a note) puts it back (`instr.leave`). Drums: Space/V kick, F/J snare, X ghost, D/K hats, S/L open hat, R/T/G/H toms, Q/P crash, W/O ride, Shift accents, 1–5 a groove (Rock, Disco, Halftime, Shuffle, Punk) on the room's grid to play over, ↑↓ tempo, N click, B count-in. Guitar: 1–8 strum the chords of the room's key, Space/B strum down/up, Shift palm mute, A…Ä and Q…P the pentatonic, V distortion, C power/full chords, ←→ the room's key, M major/minor. Bass: 1–8 the chords' roots, the pentatonic rows. Keys: the tracker layout (Y-row and Q-row, two and a half octaves), Space sustain, ←→ octave, ↑↓ Piano/E-Piano/Orgel/Synth-Lead/Pad. A compact panel bottom left (✕ to stop, never takes the mouse) shows the map with the keys lighting up, the beat, the tempo, the click, the key and its chords, the groove, the sound. Strings ring until the next stroke damps them (a guitar is one strum at a time); a keyboard key's note ends when it comes up.

**The wire** (`shared/instruments.ts`; handled in `server/ws/handlers/instruments.ts`, kept in `server/venue/instruments.ts`, one `Instruments` per office):
- client → office: `{ t: 'instr.hello' }` (coming in), `{ t: 'instr.take', spot }`, `{ t: 'instr.leave' }`, `{ t: 'instr.note', spot, note: InstrumentNote }`, `{ t: 'instr.jam', room, change: { bpm?, click?, key?, minor?, countIn? } }`, `{ t: 'instr.tone', spot, tone }`.
- office → everyone in the venue: `{ t: 'instr.state', players: Record<spotId, peerId> }` (who plays what: on every change, and to whoever says hello), `{ t: 'instr.note', id: string /* peer */, spot: string, note: InstrumentNote }` (to everyone in the venue but the player, whose page has played it already; droppable), `{ t: 'instr.jam', jams: Partial<Record<VenueRoomId, Jam>> }` (all rooms' to whoever says hello, one room's on a change), `{ t: 'instr.tones', tones: Record<spotId, tone> }` (each spot's sound: the guitar's channel `drive`/`clean`, the keyboard's patch).
- A note is taken only from whoever holds that spot, standing within `PLAY_REACH` of it (walked off: the instrument goes back, everyone told), of the spot's own kind, pitch whole and in the instrument's range (`PITCH_RANGE`; the drums' one of `DRUM_PIECES`), `vel` 0..1, `len` 0..16 s (`cleanNote`), at most `NOTE_RATE` (a burst of 120, 80 a second) per player (`RateBucket`). `vel` 0 lets go of that pitch (a key coming up: MIDI's note-off; never the drums); a guitar or bass note with `len` ≤ 0.35 s is palm-muted. One player per instrument, one instrument per player, taken within `TAKE_REACH` (3.5 m). Leaving the venue or the office puts it back (`instrumentsHooks`). A room's jam (`Jam`: bpm, click, key, minor, the grid's downbeat `at` and the last count-in, office clock) is changed only from inside that room or by someone playing in it.
- **For the studio's recorder** (the rehearsal part): every note anyone plays reaches every page in the venue as `instr.note` (watch it with a part's `onMessage`, or `ctx.messages.on('instr.note', …)`); the office doesn't echo a note to whoever played it, so their own page hands it to its own listeners itself (`ctx.messages.dispatch`, the same message with their own id) as it sends it: a recorder hears its own player's notes like everyone else's. `instr.state` says who plays what. To play a take back, `venueSynth()?.play(note, at)` (world/venue/parts.ts): it sounds as the nearest instrument of the note's kind in `at`'s room does (its patch, its amp), from `at`, heard only in that room. Velocity-0 notes in a take let go of held keys.

**Sound** (`sound/`, on the music volume: `ctx.sound.instruments`): drums synthesized per hit (`drums.ts`: the kick a diving sine with the beater's click, the snare's shell modes and wires, toms, hats/crash/ride from inharmonic cymbal metal, the open hat choked by the closed), guitar and bass as Karplus-Strong strings (`strings.ts`: tuned to the cent, the pick's position, palm mutes; rendered per pitch into a small cache) through a guitar amp (`engine.ts`: a tight boost into a 4x-oversampled waveshaper and a 4x12 cab's curve, or a clean channel) or the bass rig, the keyboard's five patches (`keys.ts`: detuned piano strings with a hammer, FM e-piano with tremolo, drawbar organ with percussion, click and a rotating speaker's wobble, a resonant saw lead with vibrato, a breathing pad). Each spot plays into its own chain and a panner where it stands into its room's bus with its reverb (the hall 2.6 s, a rehearsal room 0.55 s, the studio 0.35 s, tails without the lows); each frame a room's bus is as loud as `heardIn(you, room)` says: a rehearsal room only inside it, the hall everywhere outside them, faint and dull (a lowpass) out in the wing's lobby and corridor. Your own notes sound at once, before they're sent. The click is in your ear only while you play in that room; the count-in is the drummer's sticks, heard by everyone in the room.

**Others** (`people.ts`): whoever plays is posed at it (`Person.setWorkout`): the drummer sitting with sticks in both hands, the hand that hit coming down, the right foot on the pedal; guitarists and bassists with the instrument strapped on, the picking hand stroking, the fretting hand moving up the neck; the keyboarder's hands going down; the singer's hand at the mic, nodding with the voice. In first person you see your sticks or your guitar (the hands' scene); the guitar leaves its stand while it's played. **The mics**: the stage's are on the PA for everyone in the hall (`Voice.setPa`, and you're on it yourself, `selfOnPa`, so your mic reaches the whole venue whatever your Hörkreis); a rehearsal room's only for whoever's in that room (`micsOnPa(listenerRoom, players)` in `shared/instruments.ts`: the rehearsal part's voice isolation between rooms otherwise stays theirs).

**Seams** (`world/venue/parts.ts`): `setVenueSynth(...)` on install; `setVenueLevel('stage', level)` every frame, an envelope follower over the stage's notes (and the synth's in the hall), for the building's light show and the show's crowd.

- Own files: `src/shared/instruments.ts` (spots by id, ranges, `cleanNote`, `RateBucket`, `Jam`/`applyJam`, `TONES`, `heardIn`, `micsOnPa`, the messages), `src/shared/instruments-play.ts` (the key maps, chords and scales, grooves), `src/server/venue/instruments.ts` (`Instruments`), `src/server/ws/handlers/instruments.ts` (`instrumentsHandlers`, `instrumentsHooks`), `src/client/features/instruments/` (`index.ts`, `stage.ts`, `stations.ts`, `play.ts`, `people.ts`, `overlay.ts` + `overlay.css`, `gear/kit.ts`, `gear/strings.ts`, `gear/amps.ts`, `gear/keys.ts`, `sound/engine.ts`, `sound/drums.ts`, `sound/strings.ts`, `sound/keys.ts`, `sound/fx.ts`), `src/client/lab/instruments.html` + `instruments.ts` + `instruments-audio.ts` (a lab page: `view=stage|wide|kit|backline|guitar|keys|mics|room|room2|top|steps|fp-drums|fp-guitar|fp-bass|fp-keys`, `play=1` with a band at it; `audio=guitar|clean|lead|drums|bass|band|keys-piano|keys-epiano|keys-organ|keys-lead|keys-pad` renders the take offline through the same engine, draws its spectrogram and leaves a WAV in `window.__wav`), `tests/instruments.test.ts` (notes, the rate, who plays what, walking off, the jams and tones, the handlers through a little office: the venue hears, outside doesn't, late joiners, leaving; who hears which room, the PA's mics, the key maps, chords, leads, grooves; guests and party guests).
- Hook lines: `src/shared/protocol.ts` (`InstrumentsClientMsg`/`InstrumentsServerMsg` in the unions), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`...instrumentsHandlers`, `instrumentsHooks` in `features`), `src/server/guests.ts` (the six `instr.*` in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (`instr.state`, `instr.note`, `instr.jam`, `instr.tones` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installInstruments`, `__instruments`), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`instruments`), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/controls.md`, `docs/features.md`.
### The rehearsal wing

The Schallwerk's (`shared/venue.ts`) west wing, `ZONES.wing` (x -23.7..-11, the whole depth), a part of its own joined with `addVenuePart` (`client/world/venue/parts.ts`), like the bowling centre's mini golf room. In through `WING_DOOR` from the foyer.

**What's there** (`shared/proberaum-layout.ts` lays it out: `WING_SOLIDS`, `WING_SPOTS`, the doors, the ceiling at 3.3 m; `tests/proberaum-plan.test.ts` checks it): the **lobby** (north, z -15.7..-9): a red-and-cream checkerboard floor, walls plastered with stickers (fictional bands only, `posters.ts`), the **Belegungsplan** (a whiteboard with magnets: every room, free or the band till when), the **Schwarzes Brett** with flyers ("Drummer sucht Band", tear-off strips) and everyone's notes, the **polaroid wall** (every band that booked a room, a cartoon snapshot each), the **Bandname-Generator** chalkboard by the door, six lockers, a drinks machine (Mate, Spezi, Cola), the **Saitenautomat**, the **Backline-Verleih** counter with amps and cables on the shelves, the **tip jar**, a sofa corner round a coffee table under a smashed guitar ("Gig 2019 – R.I.P."), a kicker, PROBEN BIS DER ARZT KOMMT in pink neon. The **corridor** (`WING_CORRIDOR`): rubber floor with a yellow line, tubes, a cable tray, posters, a fire extinguisher; each room's **door** a heavy acoustic door (rubber seal, a little window lit by the room's light, a lever), hinged on the south jamb and swinging in, a red **PROBE LÄUFT** lightbox over it (on while the room's booked, records, plays back or anyone's played in it in the last 4 s; the studio's says AUFNAHME), the room's number and a **screen** (FREI / BELEGT, the band, till when, who's knocking, REC). **Proberaum 1** the classic (grey egg-crate foam, a blue-grey carpet, the house rules, cable hooks, crates), **Proberaum 2** the cosy one (Persian carpets on every wall and the floor, fairy lights, a lava lamp, a floor lamp, a plant), **Proberaum 3** the Metal-Keller (brick, black pyramid foam, a red LED strip, a black ceiling, crates, a flight case); in each a PA on stands, the mixer table with the little recorder, a beer fridge, a sofa, the setlist whiteboard, a stopped clock. The **studio**: the live room (oak, absorbers in three colours, a skyline diffuser behind the drums, clouds, headphones on hooks, an amp) west of the glass wall at `STUDIO_REGIE_X` (doorway at its north end), the control room east of it: the big desk facing the glass with meter bridge, near-fields and the session screen (the take's notes per instrument, the playhead), a rack, a leather couch, a mini fridge with an espresso machine, a gold record, AUFNAHME over the glass both ways. Everything keeps 0.8 m off every instrument spot (the instruments part builds those), off the doors' swings and the way in; every room, door, thing and instrument can be walked to (a grid walk in the test). About 110 draw calls for the whole wing (static parts merged, `props.ts` `bake`).

**Booking and doors** (`server/venue/proberaum.ts`, `Proberaum`): E at the Belegungsplan or a door's screen opens the booking window (`ui.ts`): book a room for 30, 60 or 120 minutes with a band name (🎲 rolls one), one booking a person, extend from now, let go; whoever's in the room when it's booked is in the band, the booker adds people in the room or takes them off. A booking runs out at its time, or when nobody of the band has been in the Schallwerk for 15 minutes. Doors are shared (`probe.door`): while a room's free anyone opens and shuts it; while it's booked only the band (or someone inside, to get out) opens it, and on everyone else's page the doorway stays shut even when it's open. Not your band: E at the door **knocks** (everyone inside hears it, the band gets a toast and sees who on the screen and in the window); E at the door from inside (or "reinlassen" in the window) lets the knocker in: in the band, the door opens. Guests and party guests may do all of it. The office checks every door and booking against where you stand (`DOOR_REACH`, `venueRoomAt`).

**Sound kept in.** Voice: `features/voicerange/walls.ts` is a seam for walls voice doesn't get through (`addVoiceWall(fn)`, `voiceWalled(a, b)`); the wing adds `soundApart` (`shared/proberaum.ts`): both in the Schallwerk, in different rooms by `venueRoomAt`, at least one of them in a rehearsal room. Then the gate doesn't send your voice to them (not even on a PA, `selfOnPa`) and their voice plays at 0. So the band hears each other, nobody outside hears them, and the hall's PA singer doesn't reach them. Instruments: the instruments part keeps each note to its room; the wing adds a thump through the door outside (`thump` for the drums, a low `tone` for the rest, louder with the door open, no cymbals). For the hall's music in a room, **`muffleFor(listenerRoom, from = 'hall', doorOpen = false)`** (`shared/proberaum.ts`; `client/features/proberaum/muffle.ts` adds `listenerRoom(floor, x, z)` and `applyMuffle(filter, gain, muffle, t)`): a gain and a low-pass cutoff, 0.16 / 260 Hz in a room with its door shut (0.4 / 1100 Hz open); the stage instruments, the DJ and the PA put them on their output (`__proberaum.doorOpen(room)` for the door). **Not wired yet, for the integrator**: the instruments' and the show's hall output through `applyMuffle(..., muffleFor(listenerRoom(store.floor, x, z), 'hall', doorOpen))`.

**The recorders** (`server/venue/proberaum-rec.ts`, `Recorders`; window `recui.ts`): E at a room's mixer (the studio's desk): ● Aufnahme with a tempo, a click and a count-in (4 beats, `playback.ts` lays the clicks on the audio clock for everyone in the room), optionally over the selected take (it plays along and ends up in the new one); ■ Stopp & behalten (named, or "<band> – Take n") or Verwerfen. The office records: it listens in on everything sent with `ctx.toVenue` (`ws/handlers/proberaum.ts` wraps it once, `wingOf`) and keeps each `instr.note` ({ spot, note }) of a recording room as `[ms, spot, pitch, vel, len]`; an instruments part sending its notes another way calls `heardInVenue(ctx, msg)`. A take stops at 10 minutes or 4000 notes, or when the room's empty; each room keeps its last 20 (`<dataDir>/proberaum-takes.json`, 0600). ▶ plays a take for everyone in the room (`probe.playing` with the notes and a start 0.6 s ahead on the office's clock; every page in the room sounds them at their spots through `venueSynth()`), 🔁 loops it (30 minutes at most, or till the room's empty) to jam over; who made a take renames or deletes it. While the room's booked only the band records and plays. The timeline in the window shows the take (or the recording, live) a lane an instrument, with the playhead.

**The rest**: the setlist on each room's whiteboard (E, shared, 16 lines / 600 characters, the band's while booked), notes on the Schwarzes Brett (140 characters, three each, 30 in all, your own taken down), the polaroid wall (24 bands), the tip jar (a coin a click, counted), all in `<dataDir>/proberaum.json` (0600) with the bookings. The fridges hand over a Helles (a Spezi once you've had enough; the studio's a Mate), the drinks machine Mate, Spezi or Cola (`ProberaumDeps.booze`, `reach`), the string machine and the backline counter a toast, the lockers a find, the kicker a score, the lava lamp a new colour. Inside the wing the camera keeps to the room you're in (`ctx.player.room` set to the lobby, the corridor or the room, and given back on the way out).

- Own files: `src/shared/proberaum.ts` (the rules, `soundApart`, `muffleFor`, `bandName`, `cleanText`, takes, the views, the messages `ProberaumClientMsg`/`ProberaumServerMsg`, `PROBE_CLIENT_MSGS`/`PROBE_SERVER_MSGS`), `src/shared/proberaum-layout.ts`, `src/server/venue/proberaum.ts`, `src/server/venue/proberaum-rec.ts`, `src/server/ws/handlers/proberaum.ts` (`proberaumHandlers`, `proberaumHooks`, `wingOf`, `heardInVenue`), `src/client/features/proberaum/` (`index.ts` install, the part, the kind `proberaum` and `Interactable.probe`, `__proberaum.act/hintAt`; `world.ts`, `shell.ts`, `doors.ts`, `lobby.ts`, `rooms.ts`, `studio.ts`, `props.ts`, `paint.ts`, `posters.ts`, `boards.ts`, `actions.ts`, `playback.ts`, `muffle.ts`, `ui.ts`, `recui.ts`, `ui.css`, `sound.ts`), `src/client/features/voicerange/walls.ts`, `src/client/lab/wing.html` + `wing.ts` (a lab page: `view=entry|lobby|lobby2|corridor|door|probe1|probe2|probe3|back1|studio|control|top`, `booked=1`, `open=1`), `tests/proberaum-plan.test.ts`, `tests/proberaum-server.test.ts`.
- Hook lines: `src/shared/protocol.ts` (`ProberaumClientMsg`/`ProberaumServerMsg` in the unions), `src/server/ws/handlers/index.ts` (`...proberaumHandlers`, `proberaumHooks` in `features`), `src/server/guests.ts` (`...PROBE_CLIENT_MSGS` in `GUEST`), `src/server/party.ts` (`...PROBE_SERVER_MSGS` in `PARTY_SEES`), `src/client/main.ts` (`installProberaum`, `__proberaum`), `src/client/sound/index.ts` (`proberaum(kind, at, strength, delay, pitch)`), `src/client/features/voicerange/index.ts` (the gate asks `voiceWalled`), `src/client/features/peers/index.ts` (a walled voice at 0), `src/client/ui/help.ts` (a row), `docs/features.md`, `docs/controls.md`.
### The sky bar's an L

The rooftop bar's counter turns at its north end (the pool's end) and runs east to the edge of the roof, so it's closed there; three more stools stand in front of that short leg (`roof-stool-7..9`), with a fourth `bar` interactable, a foot rail and the glow strip along it, and the pergola reaches over them. The bartender gets in and out at the south end, by the bungee jetty, and goes round behind the short leg when someone orders there (or now and then on his own).

- Own file: `src/shared/skybar.ts` (`ROOF_BAR`, moved out of layout.ts, `ROOF_BAR_END`, `SKYBAR_STOOLS`, the bartender's path `TENDER`).
- Hooks in upstream files: `src/shared/layout.ts` (re-exports `ROOF_BAR`, `...SKYBAR_STOOLS` in `SEATING`), `src/client/features/rooftop/world.ts` (the leg, its collider and interactable, the pergola, stools 7–9, `serve(x, z)` and the bartender's walk), `src/client/features/bar/index.ts` (`serve` gets x too), `src/client/world/tower.ts` (the leg seen from outside), `src/client/ui/whereabouts.ts` ("at the bar" takes in the leg's stools), `src/shared/coaster-keepout.ts` (the sky bar's box).

### Your look follows your account

An account looks the same wherever it signs in: the office keeps each account's shirt color and look in `.agent-office/account-looks.json` (0600, by account id), saves it whenever it changes (the character window, the barber, the tattoo studio, the boutique, the optician), and hands it out with `me.profile` so a browser that's never seen you skips the character window. Arriving, the saved look wins over what the browser brought. The shared password has no account and keeps its looks per browser, as before. And the office's admins have a top of their own, the smoking jacket (`OWNER_TOP`): midnight-blue velvet with black satin shawl lapels and cuffs, one gold button over an open black shirt, a silk pocket square, gold cufflinks, black trousers with a satin stripe and patent shoes. The wardrobe only offers it to admins, and the server takes it off anyone else's look (`forOwner`), saved ones too.

- Own files: `src/server/fork/looks.ts` (`AccountLooks`, `settleLook`), `src/client/world/character/person-smoking.ts`, `tests/account-looks.test.ts`.
- Hooks: `src/shared/avatar-outfit.ts` (`Smoking jacket` last in `TOP_STYLES`, `OWNER_TOP`, `forOwner`); `src/shared/protocol/accounts.ts` (`Me.profile`); `src/server/fork/roles.ts` (`profile` in `roleOf`); `src/server/fork/office.ts` (`looks`); `src/server/ws/connection.ts` and `src/server/ws/handlers/presence.ts` (`settleLook` on arriving and on `profile`); `src/client/state/persist.ts` (`accountProfile`) and `src/client/main.ts` (it, before `loadProfile`); `src/client/ui/wardrobe.ts` (the top's list cut short for non-admins); `src/client/world/character/person-outfit.ts` (the `Smoking jacket` case, shoes on `legs`, black trousers) and `person-marks.ts`/`person.ts` (`legs` passed through).

### No flickering faces

Nothing flickers when you move: no two faces of different colour share a plane where they overlap (z-fighting), outside or in the places. Signs, logos, stripes, trim and decals stand a centimetre or two proud of what they sit on, or the part behind stops a centimetre short; parts nobody can see (inside a wall, never drawn) are left as they were. And the renderer uses a reversed depth buffer where the GPU has `EXT_clip_control`, so faces a centimetre apart stay apart from across the city or the roof too (three falls back to the ordinary buffer elsewhere).

- Hooks in upstream files: `src/client/core/scene.ts` (`reversedDepthBuffer: true` in `makeRenderer`); one-line offsets in `src/client/world/tower.ts` (cornice, roof housing), `src/client/world/office/shell.ts` (`CORNER_IN`, baseboards at the corners), `src/client/world/office/balcony.ts` (exit stairs, the smoke-break sign), `src/client/world/office/loft.ts` (stairs, the wall over the door), `src/client/world/elevator.ts` (door seam, brass frame), `src/client/world/outside.ts` (the garage's back wall), `src/client/world/scenic/road.ts` (signs, billboard), `src/client/world/holiday.ts` (ribbons, the R.I.P. label), `src/client/features/bookshelf/world.ts` (back panel), `src/client/features/cabinet/world.ts` (bezel, screen). On a sync, keep the offsets.

### Fork maintenance

- `FORK.md` (this file), `.github/workflows/upstream-sync.yml`, `bin/update-office.sh`, and one line at the end of `CLAUDE.md` pointing here.
- `src/server/decor.ts`: the image proxy's user agent names this fork.
- `tests/fork-sizes.ts`: `FORK_CEILINGS` for upstream's size guard (`tests/size.test.ts`, 600 lines a file): how long the upstream files carrying hook lines may be, and the ceilings of the fork's own files that were already over the budget. Hooked into `tests/size.test.ts` with two lines; like upstream's list, it only gets shorter (a file gone or back within the budget has to come off it). Any other file over 600 lines fails the guard: split it.
